# List 7

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>despicable</b></td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemptible</td><td>/kənˈtemptəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可鄙的，卑劣的</td><td>contemptible behavior<br>卑劣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating a blind beggar is a contemptible thing to do.  欺骗盲丐是可鄙的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detestable</td><td>/dɪˈtɛstəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a detestable habit<br>令人厌恶的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the general's casual cruelty absolutely detestable.  他觉得那位将军随意的残暴极其可憎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonorable</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>a dishonorable discharge<br>不光彩的除役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Betraying an old friend for money is plainly dishonorable.  为钱背叛老友显然可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 admirable</td><td>/ˈædmərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人钦佩的；极好的</td><td>admirable courage<br>令人钦佩的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patience with difficult students was truly admirable.  她对难教学生的耐心确实令人钦佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendable</td><td>/kəˈmendəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>commendable honesty<br>值得赞扬的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team showed commendable calm during the long night.  救援队在漫长的夜里表现出值得赞扬的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 creditable</td><td>/ˈkredətəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得称赞的；相当好的</td><td>a creditable performance<br>值得称赞的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist gave a creditable account of the concerto.  年轻小提琴手对协奏曲的演绎值得称赞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meritorious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有功的，值得称赞的</td><td>meritorious service<br>有功的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for meritorious conduct under heavy fire.  他因在猛烈炮火下的有功表现获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praiseworthy</td><td>/ˈpreɪzˌwɜːrði/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a praiseworthy effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their decision to return the money was entirely praiseworthy.  他们归还钱款的决定完全值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>despise</b></td><td>/dɪˈspaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鄙视，极其不喜欢</td><td>despise a coward<br>鄙视懦夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She despised him for abandoning his closest friends.  她鄙视他抛弃至交的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abhor</td><td>/əbˈhɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>极度厌恶，深恶痛绝</td><td>abhor violence<br>痛恨暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She abhors any form of cruelty to animals.  她极度厌恶任何形式的虐待动物行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abominate</del></td><td>/əˈbɑːmɪneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>痛恨，厌恶</td><td>abominate cruelty<br>憎恶残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He abominated the hypocrisy he saw all around him.  他痛恨周围随处可见的虚伪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detest</td><td>/dɪˈtest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>厌恶，憎恨</td><td>detest hypocrisy<br>厌恶虚伪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I detest waiting in long lines on hot days.  我讨厌在炎热的日子里排长队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 execrate</del></td><td>/ˈeksɪkreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>痛恨，咒骂</td><td>execrate a tyrant<br>痛斥暴君</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians still execrate the tyrant for his cruelty.  史学家仍因其残暴而痛斥这个暴君。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loathe</td><td>/loʊð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>厌恶，讨厌</td><td>loathe the sight of<br>一见就厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He loathes the smell of boiled cabbage.  他讨厌煮卷心菜的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flout</td><td>/flaʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄性不理会，蔑视</td><td>flout the rules<br>蔑视规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers who flout the rules face heavy fines this year.  今年蔑视规则的司机将面临重罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 love</td><td>/ˈlʌv/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>爱，热爱；喜爱</td><td>fall in love with<br>爱上，与…相爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I love walking along the river in the early morning.  我喜欢清晨沿着河边散步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>despotic</b></td><td>/dɪˈspɑːtɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专制的，暴虐的</td><td>a despotic regime<br>专制的政权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country suffered under a despotic regime for decades.  这个国家在专制政权下受苦数十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authoritative</td><td>/əˈθɔːrəˌteɪtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专断的，命令式的；权威的</td><td>an authoritative tone<br>专断的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She silenced the room with one authoritative wave of her hand.  她专断地一挥手就让全场安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dictatorial</td><td>/ˌdɪktəˈtɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>独裁的；专横的</td><td>a dictatorial manner<br>专横的作风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach's dictatorial manner made several players quit.  教练专横的态度让几名球员退出了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overbearing</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈberɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的</td><td>an overbearing boss<br>专横傲慢的老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overbearing manner made junior colleagues afraid to voice any objection.  他专横的作风让年轻同事不敢提出任何反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 tyrannous</del></td><td>/ˈtɪrənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横的，暴虐的</td><td>tyrannous rule<br>暴虐的统治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The colony finally rebelled against a tyrannous foreign governor.  这块殖民地终于反抗暴虐的外来总督。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desultory</td><td>/ˈdesəlˌtɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>散漫的，无条理的</td><td>desultory conversation<br>散漫的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They made desultory notes instead of a real research plan.  他们做了些散漫的笔记，而非真正的研究计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 digressive</td><td>/daɪˈɡrɛsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>离题的，枝节的</td><td>a digressive lecture<br>离题的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His digressive style made the short talk feel endless.  他离题的讲法让短短的讲话显得没完没了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 excursive</del></td><td>/ɪkˈskɜːrsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>离题的；散漫的</td><td>an excursive narrative<br>散漫的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His excursive lecture wandered far from the announced topic.  他的讲座散漫离题，远离了预告的主题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meandering</td><td>/miˈændərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>曲折的；散漫的</td><td>a meandering speech<br>散漫的讲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The meandering path followed every bend of the small stream.  这条曲折的小径顺着小溪的每个弯道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rambling</td><td>/ˈræmblɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的，散漫的</td><td>a rambling explanation<br>散漫的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a rambling answer that satisfied nobody present.  他给出散漫的回答，在场无人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wandering</td><td>/ˈwɑːndərɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>游荡的；散漫的</td><td>a wandering discussion<br>散漫的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wandering argument never reached any clear conclusion.  这番散漫的论述始终没有明确结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 carefully planned</td><td>/ˈkɛrfəli ˈplænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精心策划的</td><td>a carefully planned route<br>精心规划的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The escape was carefully planned over many patient months.  这次出逃经过好几个月耐心的精心策划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 strictly methodical</td><td>/ˈstrɪktli məˈθɑdɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极有条理的</td><td>strictly methodical research<br>极有条理的研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her strictly methodical filing let her find anything instantly.  她极有条理的归档让她能立刻找到任何东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assiduous</td><td>/əˈsɪdʒuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，专心仔细的</td><td>assiduous attention to detail<br>对细节的专注仔细</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her assiduous attention to detail saved the project from disaster.  她对细节的专注仔细使项目免于失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>detach</b></td><td>/dɪˈtætʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使分离</td><td>detach the coupon carefully<br>小心撕下优惠券</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Carefully detach the label before you put the jar in water.  把罐子放进水里之前先小心地把标签撕下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tether</td><td>/ˈteðər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（用绳、铁链）拴系，束缚</td><td>tether a goat outside<br>把山羊拴在外面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer tethered a goat outside the barn every morning.  农夫每天早上把山羊拴在谷仓外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>detain</b></td><td>/dɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拘留，扣押；留住</td><td>detain a suspect<br>拘留嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police detained the two men for questioning about the missing car.  警方拘留了这两名男子，就失踪汽车一事问话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 manumit</del></td><td>/ˌmænjuˈmɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放（奴隶）</td><td>manumit his slaves<br>解放他的奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dying planter chose to manumit every slave who had worked his fields.  这位垂死的种植园主决定解放所有为他耕作过的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deter</b></td><td>/dɪˈtɜːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吓住，威慑</td><td>deter potential criminals<br>威慑潜在罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Better street lighting may deter thieves from working in this neighborhood.  更好的街道照明或许能威慑小偷不在这一带作案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissuade</td><td>/dɪˈsweɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>劝阻，劝止</td><td>dissuade him from leaving<br>劝他不要离开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His parents tried to dissuade him from leaving school before the final term.  他父母试图劝他不要在最后一学期前退学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhibit</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，阻碍</td><td>inhibit the growth of bacteria<br>抑制细菌生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold weather inhibits the growth of these tropical plants.  寒冷天气抑制了这些热带植物的生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spur</td><td>/spɜːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激物</td><td>a spur to growth<br>增长的刺激因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising exports acted as a powerful spur to industrial growth.  出口增长成为工业发展的强大刺激因素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 persuade</td><td>/pərˈsweɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>说服，劝服</td><td>persuade her to stay<br>说服她留下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing could persuade the stubborn old farmer to sell.  没什么能说服这位固执的老农出售土地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inducement</td><td>/ɪnˈduːsmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诱因，刺激物</td><td>a financial inducement<br>金钱上的诱因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Free housing was the main inducement for teachers to move.  免费住房是吸引教师迁来的主要诱因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incentive</td><td>/ɪnˈsentɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激，诱因</td><td>a tax incentive<br>税收上的激励</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tax breaks gave farmers a strong incentive to plant native trees.  税收减免给了农民种植本土树木的强烈诱因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deterioration</b></td><td>/dɪˌtɪriəˈreɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶化；堕落</td><td>a sharp deterioration in health<br>健康急剧恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors noted a sharp deterioration in the old man's health.  医生注意到这位老人的健康急剧恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decadence</td><td>/ˈdekədəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>衰落，颓废</td><td>signs of moral decadence<br>道德颓废的迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The historian blamed the empire's fall on decades of quiet decadence.  这位历史学家把帝国的覆亡归因于数十年的悄然衰颓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degeneration</td><td>/dɪˌdʒenəˈreɪʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>退化，衰退</td><td>the degeneration of nerve cells<br>神经细胞退化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors monitored the gradual degeneration of the patient's eyesight.  医生监测着病人视力的逐渐衰退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devolution</td><td>/ˌdevəˈluːʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>退化，衰退；权力下移</td><td>the devolution of standards<br>标准的退化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics blamed the devolution of the service on constant cuts.  批评者把服务水平的退化归咎于不断削减。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downfall</td><td>/ˈdaʊnˌfɔːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>垮台，衰落</td><td>lead to his downfall<br>导致他垮台</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Greed eventually led to the powerful banker's sudden downfall.  贪婪最终导致这位有权势的银行家突然垮台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downgrade</td><td>/ˈdaʊnɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>降级，降低等级</td><td>downgrade the credit rating<br>下调信用评级</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Analysts downgraded the company after two weak quarterly reports.  两份疲弱季报后分析师下调了该公司评级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 improvement</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːvmənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>改进，改善</td><td>a marked improvement<br>明显的改善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new coach brought a marked improvement in team defense.  新教练让球队防守有了明显改善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recovery</td><td>/rɪˈkʌvəri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复，康复；复苏</td><td>a slow recovery<br>缓慢的康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors expect a full recovery within about three months.  医生预计约三个月内完全康复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recuperation</td><td>/rɪˌkuːpəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复，休养</td><td>a period of recuperation<br>一段休养期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the summer in quiet recuperation beside the sea.  他在海边安静休养度过整个夏天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rehabilitation</td><td>/ˌriːhəbɪləˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>修复，复原；康复</td><td>the rehabilitation of the building<br>这座建筑的修复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old mill's rehabilitation took four years and much money.  这座旧磨坊的修复花了四年和许多钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revitalization</td><td>/ˌriːvaɪtələˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复兴，重新振兴</td><td>urban revitalization<br>城市复兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The festival began the revitalization of the sleepy river town.  这个节庆开启了沉寂河镇的复兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>detour</b></td><td>/ˈdiːtʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>绕道，迂路；偏离</td><td>take a long detour<br>绕一段远路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Roadworks forced us to take a long detour through the hills.  道路施工迫使我们绕道穿越丘陵走了很远。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deflection</td><td>/dɪˈflekʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>偏离，偏转</td><td>a slight deflection<br>轻微的偏转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tiny deflection of the beam ruined the whole measurement.  光束的微小偏离毁了整次测量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 departure</td><td>/dɪˈpɑːrtʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏离；离开，出发</td><td>a departure from tradition<br>对传统的偏离</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ruling marks a sharp departure from earlier court practice.  该裁决明显偏离了此前的法院惯例。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviation</td><td>/ˌdiːviˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>偏离，背离</td><td>a deviation from the norm<br>对常规的偏离</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Any deviation from the standard procedure must be reported immediately.  任何对标准流程的偏离都必须立即上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 divergency</del></td><td>/daɪˈvɜrdʒənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，偏离</td><td>a divergency of opinion<br>意见上的分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A slight divergency in the two accounts troubled the judge.  两份陈述的细微分歧让法官不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>detract</b></td><td>/dɪˈtrækt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>贬低，降低价值；使分心</td><td>detract from the value<br>降低其价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ugly frame detracts from the painting.  难看的画框降低了这幅画的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belittle</td><td>/bɪˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低，轻视</td><td>belittle her achievements<br>贬低她的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't belittle her achievements just because she works quietly.  别因为她默默工作就贬低她的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depreciate</td><td>/dɪˈpriːʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低…的价值</td><td>depreciate his contribution<br>贬低他的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not depreciate the value of patience in such long negotiations.  不要贬低耐心在这样漫长谈判中的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derogate</td><td>/ˈderəɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>贬损，诋毁</td><td>derogate from an achievement<br>贬损某项成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing should derogate from her remarkable achievement here.  什么都不应贬损她在这里的非凡成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disparage</td><td>/dɪˈspærɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低；轻蔑地说</td><td>disparage someone's efforts<br>贬低某人的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not disparage her work simply because she is young.  不要仅仅因为她年轻就贬低她的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dispraise</del></td><td>/dɪˈspreɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>贬低，责难</td><td>dispraise a rival's work<br>贬低对手的作品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers dispraised the novel for its clumsy final chapter.  评论者因结尾笨拙而贬低这部小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 write off</td><td>/raɪt ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>否定，认为无价值；注销</td><td>write off the whole plan<br>否定整个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Coaches wrote off the young rookie far too early.  教练们过早地否定了这名年轻新人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divert</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使转向；使消遣</td><td>divert attention from<br>转移对…的注意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise diverted my attention from the lecture.  噪音把我的注意力从讲座上引开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 call off</td><td>/kɔl ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取消；叫走，使离开</td><td>call off the search<br>取消搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called off the search because of the storm.  因暴风雨他们取消了搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 panegyrize</del></td><td>/ˈpænɪdʒəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>赞颂，写颂词</td><td>panegyrize a fallen leader<br>赞颂逝去的领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Court poets panegyrized the emperor in long ceremonial odes.  宫廷诗人以长篇颂歌赞颂皇帝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>detritus</b></td><td>/dɪˈtraɪtəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>废品，碎屑，遗骸</td><td>the detritus of war<br>战争留下的残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The beach was littered with the detritus of the summer crowds.  海滩上散落着夏日人群留下的垃圾碎屑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debris</td><td>/dəˈbriː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>碎片，残骸</td><td>clear away the debris<br>清理废墟碎片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rescuers dug through the debris for hours looking for survivors.  救援人员在废墟中挖掘了数小时，寻找生还者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 residue</td><td>/ˈrezɪduː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>剩余物</td><td>a sticky residue<br>黏稠的残留物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dark residue remained at the bottom of the flask.  烧瓶底部留下了深色的残留物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wreck</td><td>/ˈrek/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>残骸；失事；破坏</td><td>the wreck of a ship<br>一艘船的残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers photographed the wreck lying deep on the sandy bottom.  潜水员拍摄了深卧沙底的残骸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valuable product</td><td>/ˈvæljəbəl ˈprɑdəkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>有价值的产品</td><td>a valuable product line<br>有价值的产品线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refinery turns waste gas into a valuable product.  炼厂把废气变成有价值的产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>devoted</b></td><td>/dɪˈvoʊtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>投入的；忠诚的</td><td>a devoted follower<br>忠诚的追随者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a devoted teacher who remembers every student by name.  她是位投入的教师，能叫出每个学生的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loyal</td><td>/ˈlɔɪəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，忠实的</td><td>a loyal customer<br>忠实的客户</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained loyal to the team even during its worst season.  即使在最糟的赛季，他仍然忠于这支球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allegiant</td><td>/əˈlidʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，效忠的</td><td>allegiant to the cause<br>忠于事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old guard stayed allegiant to the founder until his death.  元老们一直忠于创始人，直到他去世。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dedicated</td><td>/ˈdedəkeɪtəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>献身的，尽心的；专用的</td><td>a dedicated teacher<br>尽心尽力的教师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is dedicated to protecting the birds of this marsh.  她致力于保护这片沼泽的鸟类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devout</td><td>/dɪˈvaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虔诚的，笃信的</td><td>a devout believer<br>虔诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His devout aunt attended the little chapel every single morning.  他虔诚的姑妈每天早晨都去那间小教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pious</td><td>/ˈpaɪəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（信仰上）虔诚的</td><td>a pious old woman<br>虔诚的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pious old woman attended the early service every single morning.  那位虔诚的老妇人每天早上都参加早祷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 staunch</td><td>/stɔːntʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，坚定的</td><td>a staunch supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A staunch supporter of reform, she never wavered under public pressure.  作为改革的坚定支持者，她从未在公众压力下动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steadfast</td><td>/ˈstedfæst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，忠诚的</td><td>steadfast in his belief<br>信念坚定不移</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained steadfast in her belief despite years of public ridicule.  尽管多年遭受公众嘲笑，她依然坚定地坚持自己的信念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disloyal</td><td>/dɪˈslɔɪəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，背信的</td><td>disloyal to a friend<br>对朋友不忠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sharing those emails was disloyal to everyone on the team.  分享那些邮件是对团队所有人的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faithless</td><td>/ˈfeɪθləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，背信的</td><td>a faithless ally<br>不忠的盟友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king punished his faithless advisers without any mercy.  国王毫不留情地惩罚了背信的谋臣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfidious</td><td>/pərˈfɪdiəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>背信弃义的，奸诈的</td><td>a perfidious friend<br>背信弃义的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a perfidious partner in every treaty.  历史记住他是每份条约中背信弃义的伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfaithful</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪθfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，不忠实的</td><td>an unfaithful servant<br>不忠的仆人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proved unfaithful to the promises he had already signed.  他背弃了自己已签下的承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>devout</b></td><td>/dɪˈvaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虔诚的，笃信的</td><td>a devout believer<br>虔诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His devout aunt attended the little chapel every single morning.  他虔诚的姑妈每天早晨都去那间小教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loyal</td><td>/ˈlɔɪəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，忠实的</td><td>a loyal customer<br>忠实的客户</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained loyal to the team even during its worst season.  即使在最糟的赛季，他仍然忠于这支球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allegiant</td><td>/əˈlidʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，效忠的</td><td>allegiant to the cause<br>忠于事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old guard stayed allegiant to the founder until his death.  元老们一直忠于创始人，直到他去世。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dedicated</td><td>/ˈdedəkeɪtəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>献身的，尽心的；专用的</td><td>a dedicated teacher<br>尽心尽力的教师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is dedicated to protecting the birds of this marsh.  她致力于保护这片沼泽的鸟类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pious</td><td>/ˈpaɪəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（信仰上）虔诚的</td><td>a pious old woman<br>虔诚的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pious old woman attended the early service every single morning.  那位虔诚的老妇人每天早上都参加早祷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 staunch</td><td>/stɔːntʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，坚定的</td><td>a staunch supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A staunch supporter of reform, she never wavered under public pressure.  作为改革的坚定支持者，她从未在公众压力下动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steadfast</td><td>/ˈstedfæst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，忠诚的</td><td>steadfast in his belief<br>信念坚定不移</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained steadfast in her belief despite years of public ridicule.  尽管多年遭受公众嘲笑，她依然坚定地坚持自己的信念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disloyal</td><td>/dɪˈslɔɪəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，背信的</td><td>disloyal to a friend<br>对朋友不忠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sharing those emails was disloyal to everyone on the team.  分享那些邮件是对团队所有人的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faithless</td><td>/ˈfeɪθləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，背信的</td><td>a faithless ally<br>不忠的盟友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king punished his faithless advisers without any mercy.  国王毫不留情地惩罚了背信的谋臣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfidious</td><td>/pərˈfɪdiəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>背信弃义的，奸诈的</td><td>a perfidious friend<br>背信弃义的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a perfidious partner in every treaty.  历史记住他是每份条约中背信弃义的伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfaithful</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪθfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，不忠实的</td><td>an unfaithful servant<br>不忠的仆人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proved unfaithful to the promises he had already signed.  他背弃了自己已签下的承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dexterous</b></td><td>/ˈdekstrəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动作灵活的；头脑灵活的</td><td>dexterous fingers<br>灵巧的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The surgeon's dexterous hands worked steadily for more than six hours.  这位外科医生灵活的双手稳稳地工作了六个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deft</td><td>/deft/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵巧的，熟练的</td><td>a deft touch<br>娴熟的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a few deft strokes the artist finished the small sketch.  画家几笔灵巧的勾画便完成了那幅小素描。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handy</td><td>/ˈhændi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>方便的；就在手边的</td><td>come in handy<br>派上用场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep a flashlight handy in case the power fails tonight.  把手电筒放在手边，以防今晚停电。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adroit</td><td>/əˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，灵巧的</td><td>adroit at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved remarkably adroit at handling hostile questions from reporters.  她在应对记者的敌意提问时表现得非常娴熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cunning</td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a cunning scheme<br>狡猾的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fox was cunning enough to avoid every trap they set.  这只狐狸够狡猾，避开了他们设的每个陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ham-fisted</td><td>/ˈhæm ˈfɪstɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，拙劣的</td><td>a ham-fisted attempt<br>拙劣的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ham-fisted repair left the old clock worse than before.  他笨拙的修理让旧钟比之前更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ham-handed</td><td>/ˌhæm ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，不圆滑的</td><td>a ham-handed apology<br>笨拙的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ham-handed attempt to help only made things worse.  他笨拙的帮忙只让事情更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handless</td><td>/ˈhændləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，不灵巧的</td><td>handless with tools<br>使用工具很笨拙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is completely handless in the kitchen and burns almost everything.  他在厨房里笨手笨脚，几乎什么都烧糊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heavy-handed</td><td>/ˌhevi ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的；严厉专断的</td><td>heavy-handed policing<br>粗暴的执法</td></tr>

</tbody>

<tr><td><del>↳反 unhandy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhændi/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不便使用的；笨手笨脚的</td><td>an unhandy tool<br>不好用的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This unhandy old ladder is difficult to carry upstairs.  这把不好用的旧梯子很难搬上楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 silly</td><td>/ˈsɪli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a silly mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was silly to climb that tall ladder without help.  没人帮忙就爬那么高的梯子太傻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fatuous</td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwise</td><td>/ʌnˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，愚蠢的</td><td>an unwise decision<br>不明智的决定</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diabolic</b></td><td>/ˌdaɪəˈbɑːlɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔一般的</td><td>a diabolic scheme<br>恶魔般的阴谋</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demoniac</td><td>/dɪˈmoʊniæk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>恶魔般的；着魔的</td><td>demoniac laughter<br>恶魔般的笑声</td></tr>

<tr><td><del>↳近 demonian</del></td><td>/dɪˈmoʊniən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>魔鬼的，恶魔似的</td><td>demonian cruelty<br>恶魔似的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers spoke of a demonian shape haunting the burned mill.  村民谈起一个恶魔似的影子出没于烧毁的磨坊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demonic</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːnɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔般的；狂热的</td><td>demonic energy<br>狂热的精力</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 satanic</td><td>/səˈtænɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔的；邪恶的</td><td>satanic rituals<br>邪恶的仪式</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 angelic</td><td>/ænˈdʒelɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天使般的；纯洁善良的</td><td>an angelic smile<br>天使般的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child sang with an angelic clarity that hushed everyone.  孩子的歌声清澈如天使，让所有人安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳反 seraphic</td><td>/səˈræfɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天使般的；纯洁的</td><td>a seraphic expression<br>天使般的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She listened with a seraphic calm that surprised the room.  她带着天使般的平静聆听，令全场惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diaphanous</b></td><td>/daɪˈæfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>几乎透明的；模糊的，非实在的</td><td>a diaphanous silk veil<br>轻薄透明的丝纱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore a diaphanous scarf that barely shaded her from the sun.  她戴着一条几乎透明的纱巾，几乎挡不住阳光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vague</td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unexplicit</del></td><td>/ˌʌnɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明确的，含糊的</td><td>unexplicit instructions<br>不明确的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract stayed unexplicit about who paid for repairs.  合同对谁支付修理费始终含糊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insubstantial</td><td>/ˌɪnsəbˈstænʃəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非实在的；虚弱的</td><td>insubstantial evidence<br>站不住脚的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His case rested on insubstantial rumor rather than real documents.  他的论据靠的是虚无的传言而非真实文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳近 immaterial</td><td>/ˌɪməˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>immaterial to the case<br>与本案无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether he liked the plan is immaterial to the legal question here.  他是否喜欢这个计划与这里的法律问题无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transparent</td><td>/trænsˈpærənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有歧义的，清晰易懂的</td><td>a transparent explanation<br>清晰易懂的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a transparent explanation that even beginners understood immediately.  他给出清晰易懂的解释，连初学者都立刻明白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 translucent</td><td>/trænsˈluːsnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>半透明的</td><td>translucent glass panels<br>半透明的玻璃板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Morning light glowed softly through the translucent paper screens.  晨光柔和地透过半透明的纸屏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 transpicuous</del></td><td>/trænˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>透明的；明白易懂的</td><td>transpicuous prose<br>明白易懂的文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His transpicuous explanation made the hard theorem suddenly obvious.  他明白易懂的讲解让难懂的定理忽然清晰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gossamer</td><td>/ˈɡɑːsəmər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>虚无缥缈的东西；轻薄的；薄弱的</td><td>gossamer wings<br>薄如蝉翼的翅膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her gossamer scarf floated on the breeze like a wisp of smoke.  她轻薄如蝉翼的围巾在微风中飘动，像一缕青烟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 opaque</td><td>/oʊˈpeɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透明的；晦涩难懂的</td><td>opaque legal language<br>晦涩的法律用语</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impermeable of light</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜrmiəbəl əv laɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透光的</td><td>a layer impermeable of light<br>不透光的一层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vault walls are impermeable of light and sound alike.  库房的墙壁既不透光也不透声。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diatribe</b></td><td>/ˈdaɪətraɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>launch into a diatribe<br>开始长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech turned into a long diatribe against modern popular music.  他的演讲变成了对现代流行音乐的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tirade</td><td>/ˈtaɪreɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>an angry tirade<br>愤怒的长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach launched an angry tirade against the referee's decision.  教练对裁判的判罚发起愤怒的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harangue</td><td>/həˈræŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（发表）长篇大论</td><td>a long harangue<br>长篇的训话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager harangued his staff for twenty minutes about missed deadlines.  经理就错过截止日期的事对员工发表了二十分钟的长篇训话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeremiad</td><td>/ˌdʒerəˈmaɪəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>哀诉，悲叹式的抨击文</td><td>a gloomy jeremiad<br>阴郁的哀叹长文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His column became a weekly jeremiad about modern manners.  他的专栏成了每周对现代风气的哀叹式抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 philippic</del></td><td>/fɪˈlɪpɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈的抨击演说</td><td>deliver a fierce philippic<br>发表激烈的抨击演说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's philippic against the bill lasted two full hours.  这位参议员抨击该法案的演说整整两小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rant</td><td>/rænt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>（尤指长时间的）训斥，责骂</td><td>go on a rant<br>大发一通牢骚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach went on a long rant about the team's careless defending.  教练就球队漫不经心的防守训斥了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>didactic</b></td><td>/daɪˈdæktɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜欢说教的；教诲的</td><td>a didactic tone<br>说教的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The story is charming, though its ending is rather heavily didactic.  这个故事很动人，只是结尾说教味相当重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homiletic</td><td>/ˌhɑːməˈletɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说教的，布道式的</td><td>a homiletic essay<br>说教式的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel turns homiletic whenever the narrator discusses money.  每当叙述者谈钱，小说就变得说教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preachy</td><td>/ˈpriːtʃi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱说教的</td><td>a preachy narrator<br>爱说教的叙述者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers disliked the preachy final chapter of the book.  读者不喜欢书中爱说教的最后一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moralistic</td><td>/ˌmɔːrəˈlɪstɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道德说教的</td><td>a moralistic lecture<br>道德说教的讲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her moralistic reading of the fable annoyed the students.  她对寓言的道德说教式解读让学生反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sententious</td><td>/sɛnˈtɛnʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱说教的；故作警句的</td><td>sententious remarks<br>故作警句的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge grew sententious after his second glass of wine.  老法官喝了第二杯酒后满口格言说教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undidactic</del></td><td>/ˌʌndaɪˈdæktɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不说教的</td><td>an undidactic approach<br>不说教的方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children prefer an undidactic story that simply entertains them.  孩子更喜欢单纯有趣、不说教的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diehard</b></td><td>/ˈdaɪhɑːrd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>顽固的，保守的；顽固分子</td><td>a diehard fan<br>死忠的粉丝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few diehard supporters stayed until the end of the match.  只有少数死忠支持者留到比赛结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conservative</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrvətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，不愿改变的；不招摇的，低调的</td><td>a conservative estimate<br>保守的估计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents were too conservative to accept such an unconventional career choice.  她的父母太守旧，无法接受这样不合常规的职业选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hidebound</td><td>/ˈhaɪdbaʊnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>死板的，极度保守的</td><td>hidebound traditions<br>僵化的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hidebound innkeeper flatly refused to install internet for guests.  这位死板的旅店老板断然拒绝为客人装网络。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 old-fashioned</td><td>/ˌoʊldˈfæʃənd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，过时的</td><td>old-fashioned manners<br>守旧的礼节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His old-fashioned views on marriage puzzled his own children.  他守旧的婚姻观让自己的孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reactionary</td><td>/riˈækʃəneri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>反对变革的，极保守的</td><td>reactionary policies<br>反对变革的政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the new rules reactionary and openly unfair.  批评者称新规定保守倒退且明显不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ultraconservative</td><td>/ˌʌltrəkənˈsɜːrvətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极端保守的；极端保守者</td><td>an ultraconservative faction<br>极端保守的派别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party's ultraconservative wing blocked every proposed reform.  该党极端保守的一翼阻挠了每项改革提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonconservative</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnkənˈsɜrvətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非保守的，开放的</td><td>a nonconservative outlook<br>非保守的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Younger voters favored the nonconservative candidate by wide margins.  年轻选民以巨大优势支持这位非保守派候选人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 open-minded</td><td>/ˈoʊpən ˈmaɪndɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想开明的</td><td>an open-minded editor<br>思想开明的编辑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good reviewer stays open-minded about unfamiliar musical styles.  好的评论者对陌生的音乐风格保持开明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dovish</b></td><td>/ˈdʌvɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鸽派的，爱好和平的</td><td>a dovish policy<br>鸽派政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank adopted a dovish policy and kept interest rates unusually low.  央行采取鸽派政策，把利率维持在异常低的水平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pacific</td><td>/pəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平和的，爱好和平的</td><td>a pacific temperament<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He settled the quarrel with a few pacific words.  他用几句平和的话平息了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pacifist</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>和平主义者；和平主义的</td><td>a pacifist movement<br>和平主义运动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a pacifist he refused to carry any weapon.  作为和平主义者，他拒绝携带任何武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peaceable</td><td>/ˈpiːsəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱好和平的；平和的</td><td>a peaceable neighbor<br>平和的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceable villagers settled disputes without ever calling police.  这些平和的村民解决纠纷从不报警。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bloodthirsty</td><td>/ˈblʌdθɜːrsti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>嗜杀的，残暴的</td><td>a bloodthirsty tyrant<br>嗜杀的暴君</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chronicle describes a bloodthirsty king who spared nobody.  编年史描写了一位不留活口的嗜杀国王。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hawkish</td><td>/ˈhɔːkɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>主战的，鹰派的</td><td>a hawkish foreign policy<br>鹰派的外交政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new adviser took a hawkish line on the border.  新顾问在边境问题上持鹰派立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warlike</td><td>/ˈwɔːrˌlaɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，尚武的</td><td>a warlike nation<br>好战的国家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ancient writers portrayed the tribe as fiercely warlike.  古代作家把这个部族描写得极其好战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diffuse</b></td><td>/dɪfˈjuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长散漫的；扩散的</td><td>a diffuse and wordy essay<br>散漫冗长的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument was far too diffuse to convince a careful reader.  他的论证过于散漫，无法说服细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 circumlocutory</del></td><td>/ˌsɜrkəmˈlɑkjətɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回冗赘的，绕弯子的</td><td>circumlocutory replies<br>绕弯子的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His circumlocutory answers hid a very simple refusal.  他绕弯子的答复掩盖了一个简单的拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 long-winded</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋwɪndɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长啰嗦的</td><td>a long-winded introduction<br>冗长的开场白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chairman's long-winded report emptied half the meeting room.  主席冗长的报告让会场走了一半人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolix</td><td>/ˈproʊlɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，冗长的</td><td>prolix legal prose<br>冗长的法律文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the prolix brief for burying its argument.  法官批评这份冗长的书状埋没了自己的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 verbose</td><td>/vɜːrˈboʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a verbose explanation<br>冗长的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her verbose report could have said the same thing in two pages.  她冗长的报告本可以用两页说完同样的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 windy</td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rambling</td><td>/ˈræmblɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的，散漫的</td><td>a rambling explanation<br>散漫的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a rambling answer that satisfied nobody present.  他给出散漫的回答，在场无人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spread</td><td>/spred/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>传播，散布；蔓延</td><td>spread quickly through town<br>迅速传遍全城</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors spread through the office faster than the memo did.  谣言在办公室传得比通知还快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extend</td><td>/ɪkˈstend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>延长，扩展</td><td>extend a deadline<br>延长截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They agreed to extend the deadline by two weeks.  他们同意把截止日期延长两周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disperse</td><td>/dɪˈspɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>分散，散开，驱散</td><td>disperse the crowd<br>驱散人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police asked the protesters to disperse before nightfall.  警方要求抗议者在天黑前散开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disseminate</td><td>/dɪˈsemɪneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>传播，散布（信息）</td><td>disseminate new research<br>传播新的研究成果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The society disseminated the findings to schools across the country.  学会把研究结果传播到全国各地的学校。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scatter</td><td>/ˈskætər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>散开，四散；撒播</td><td>scatter the seeds<br>把种子撒开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The birds scattered the moment the gardener opened the gate.  园丁一开门，鸟就四散飞走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concise</td><td>/kənˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a concise explanation<br>简明的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep your answer concise; the interviewer has only ten minutes.  回答要简洁，面试官只有十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pithy</td><td>/ˈpɪθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简练的，言简意赅的</td><td>a pithy remark<br>精练的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pithy summary captured the whole debate in two sentences.  她简练的总结用两句话概括了整场辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succinct</td><td>/səkˈsɪŋkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a succinct summary<br>简洁的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her succinct summary covered the entire report in three short paragraphs.  她简洁的总结用三小段概括了整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terse</td><td>/tɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁的，简明的</td><td>a terse reply<br>简洁的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His terse note said only that the deal had collapsed.  他简短的字条只说交易破裂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concentrate</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsntreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>浓缩；聚集</td><td>concentrate the solution<br>浓缩溶液</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Boil the syrup slowly in order to concentrate the solution.  慢慢熬煮糖浆以便浓缩溶液。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 center</td><td>/ˈsentər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使集中；中心</td><td>center attention on quality<br>把注意力集中于质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report centers its argument on three very clear examples.  报告把论点集中在三个非常清晰的例子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 centralize</td><td>/ˈsentrəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使集中，集权化</td><td>centralize the decision making<br>使决策集中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform centralized purchasing in a single national office.  改革把采购集中到一个全国性机构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 focus</td><td>/ˈfoʊkəs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>聚集，集中；焦点</td><td>focus attention on<br>把注意力集中于</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens focuses sunlight into a single bright point.  透镜把阳光聚集成一个明亮的点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>digress</b></td><td>/daɪˈɡres/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>脱离主题</td><td>digress from the topic<br>偏离主题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor tended to digress from the topic whenever a student asked about history.  一有学生问到历史，教授就爱偏离主题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 excurse</del></td><td>/ɪkˈskɜrs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>离题，漫谈</td><td>excurse from the topic<br>从话题上跑开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecturer excursed happily into stories about his childhood.  讲师愉快地离题讲起童年的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ramble</td><td>/ˈræmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫谈，东拉西扯；漫步</td><td>ramble on about<br>喋喋不休地扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rambled on about his childhood for a full hour.  他絮絮叨叨讲了整整一小时童年往事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diverge</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（使）分叉，散开；分歧</td><td>diverge from the path<br>偏离原有路径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two trails diverge just beyond the old wooden bridge.  两条小径在那座老木桥之后就分岔开来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 get off the subject</td><td>/ɡɛt ˈɔf ðə ˈsʌbdʒɪkt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>离题，跑题</td><td>get off the subject again<br>又一次跑题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please try not to get off the subject today.  今天请尽量不要跑题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discursive</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（谈话内容）杂乱的</td><td>a discursive account<br>杂乱的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discursive account wandered from wine to politics and never reached a point.  他杂乱的叙述从红酒扯到政治，始终没讲到重点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aside</td><td>/əˈsaɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>副词、名词</td><td>到一边，在旁边</td><td>step aside<br>让到一边</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pushed the papers aside and looked directly at me.  他把文件推到一边，直视着我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>dilapidate</b></del></td><td>/dɪˈlæpɪdeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）荒废</td><td>dilapidate an empty house<br>使空屋荒废</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of neglect had dilapidated the once elegant mansion beyond any hope of repair.  多年疏于照管使这座曾经典雅的宅邸破败得无法修复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruin</td><td>/ˈruːɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>毁坏，毁灭；废墟</td><td>ruin the harvest<br>毁掉收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of drought ruined the harvest and emptied the village granary.  数周干旱毁掉收成，村里粮仓也空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wreck</td><td>/ˈrek/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>残骸；失事；破坏</td><td>the wreck of a ship<br>一艘船的残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers photographed the wreck lying deep on the sandy bottom.  潜水员拍摄了深卧沙底的残骸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 be in use</td><td>/bi ɪn ˈjuːs/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>在使用中</td><td>still be in use<br>仍在使用中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stone bridge is still in use after four centuries.  这座石桥四百年后仍在使用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dilate</b></td><td>/daɪˈleɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）膨胀，扩大</td><td>dilate the pupils<br>使瞳孔扩大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor used drops to dilate her pupils before examining the retina.  医生先用药水扩大她的瞳孔，再检查视网膜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expand</td><td>/ɪkˈspænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，扩张；详述</td><td>expand the business<br>扩大业务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to expand into three new markets.  公司计划扩张到三个新市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amplify</td><td>/ˈæmpləˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大，增强；详述</td><td>amplify a signal<br>放大信号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Could you amplify your last remark a little?  你能把最后那句话再详述一下吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enlarge</td><td>/ɪnˈlɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，增大</td><td>enlarge the photograph<br>放大照片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They enlarged the kitchen by removing one interior wall.  他们拆掉一道内墙把厨房扩大了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contract</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌtrækt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>契约，合同；收缩；感染疾病</td><td>sign a contract<br>签订合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both parties signed the contract without reading the final clause carefully.  双方都没有仔细读最后一条就签了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dilatory</b></td><td>/ˈdɪlətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖延的，磨蹭的</td><td>dilatory tactics<br>拖延的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dilatory response to the complaint annoyed every member of the committee.  他对投诉迟迟不作回应，令委员会每位成员恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delaying</td><td>/dɪˈleɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖延的，延误的</td><td>delaying tactics<br>拖延的策略</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The defense filed one delaying motion after another.  辩方接连提出拖延性的动议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 procrastinating</td><td>/prəˈkræstəˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖拉的，一再推迟的</td><td>a procrastinating student<br>爱拖拉的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His procrastinating habits cost the team an entire week.  他拖拉的习惯让团队损失了整整一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dragging</td><td>/ˈdræɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖沓的，缓慢的</td><td>a dragging negotiation<br>拖沓的谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dragging trial exhausted witnesses on both sides.  拖沓的审判耗尽了双方证人的精力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lagging</td><td>/ˈlæɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>落后的，迟缓的</td><td>lagging sales figures<br>落后的销售数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers worried about the lagging performance of two branches.  经理们担心两家分店迟缓的业绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tardy</td><td>/ˈtɑːrdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢的，迟缓的</td><td>a tardy response<br>迟缓的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's tardy response angered customers waiting for their refunds.  公司迟缓的回应让等退款的顾客很生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapid</td><td>/ˈræpəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快速的</td><td>rapid economic growth<br>迅速的经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city saw rapid change after the railway finally arrived.  铁路终于通达后这座城市迅速变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fast</td><td>/fæst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快的</td><td>a fast runner<br>跑得快的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a fast worker who finishes every job ahead of schedule.  他做事很快，每项工作都提前完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fleet</td><td>/fliːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的</td><td>fleet of foot<br>脚步迅捷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fleet young messenger crossed the valley before dark.  这位迅捷的年轻信使天黑前穿过了山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 precipitate</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>匆忙的；促使，导致</td><td>a precipitate departure<br>匆忙的离去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His precipitate resignation surprised colleagues who expected a much longer transition.  他匆忙的辞职让原以为过渡期会长得多的同事大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rocketing</td><td>/ˈrɑːkətɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞涨的，猛升的</td><td>rocketing housing prices<br>飞涨的房价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rocketing fuel costs forced the airline to cancel routes.  飞涨的燃油成本迫使航空公司取消航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swift</td><td>/ˈswɪft/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，敏捷的</td><td>a swift response<br>迅速的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her swift decision saved the patient a great deal of pain.  她迅速的决定让病人少受许多痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hasty</td><td>/ˈheɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，仓促的；轻率的</td><td>a hasty judgment<br>轻率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hasty decision made in anger often costs far more later.  盛怒之下作出的轻率决定往往日后代价更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hurrying</td><td>/ˈhɜːriɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，赶忙的</td><td>hurrying commuters<br>匆忙的通勤者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hurrying passengers pushed past us toward the last train.  匆忙的乘客挤过我们奔向末班车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scurrying</td><td>/ˈskɜːriɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>急匆匆的，疾走的</td><td>scurrying footsteps<br>急匆匆的脚步声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We heard scurrying mice behind the thin kitchen wall.  我们听见薄薄的厨房墙后有老鼠急窜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dilettante</b></td><td>/ˌdɪləˈtænti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>浅尝辄止的业余爱好者；不专业的</td><td>a mere dilettante<br>只是业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a mere dilettante in astronomy, he owned a telescope but read little.  作为天文学上的业余涉猎者，他有望远镜却几乎不读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dabbler</td><td>/ˈdæblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浅尝辄止者，业余涉猎者</td><td>a dabbler in poetry<br>诗歌上的业余涉猎者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained a cheerful dabbler in half a dozen crafts.  他一直是好几门手艺的快乐业余涉猎者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonexpert</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɛkspɜrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>非专家，外行（的）</td><td>a nonexpert reader<br>非专业的读者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guide explains the disease clearly for nonexpert readers.  这本指南为非专业读者清楚地解释了该疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cognoscente</del></td><td>/ˌkɑnjəˈʃɛnti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鉴赏家，行家</td><td>a wine cognoscente<br>一位品酒行家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a cognoscente would notice the flaw in that varnish.  只有行家才会注意到那层漆的瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonprofessional</td><td>/ˌnɑːnprəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>非专业的；非专业人员</td><td>nonprofessional performers<br>非专业的表演者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The choir warmly welcomes nonprofessional singers of every age.  这个合唱团热情欢迎各年龄的非专业歌手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 authority</td><td>/əˈθɔːrəti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（某领域的）权威人士；管辖权，控制</td><td>an authority on ancient coins<br>古钱币方面的权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a recognized authority on ancient Greek coins.  她是古希腊钱币方面公认的权威人士。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pro</td><td>/ˈproʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>职业选手，专业人士；赞成的</td><td>turn pro at twenty<br>二十岁转为职业选手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played like a seasoned pro in her first tournament.  她首次参赛就打得像老练的职业选手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 professional</td><td>/prəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>专业的；专业人士</td><td>a professional musician<br>专业的音乐家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a professional could repair such a delicate mechanism.  只有专业人士才能修好如此精密的机构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specialist</td><td>/ˈspeʃələst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>专家，专门研究者</td><td>consult a specialist<br>咨询专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her doctor sent her to a specialist in rare blood diseases.  医生把她转诊给罕见血液病专家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diligent</b></td><td>/ˈdɪlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，辛勤的</td><td>a diligent student<br>勤奋的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A diligent editor checked every footnote before the manuscript went to the printer.  一位勤勉的编辑在书稿付印前核对了每一条脚注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assiduous</td><td>/əˈsɪdʒuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，专心仔细的</td><td>assiduous attention to detail<br>对细节的专注仔细</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her assiduous attention to detail saved the project from disaster.  她对细节的专注仔细使项目免于失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 industrious</td><td>/ɪnˈdʌstriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，刻苦的</td><td>an industrious worker<br>勤劳的工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most industrious student in the class reviewed her notes every single night.  班里最勤奋的学生每晚都复习笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sedulous</del></td><td>/ˈsedʒələs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤奋认真的</td><td>sedulous preparation<br>勤勉认真的准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sedulous preparation showed in every answer she gave the examiners.  她勤勉认真的准备体现在给考官的每个回答中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engaged</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙于…的；已订婚的</td><td>engaged in research<br>忙于研究工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was engaged in fieldwork throughout the entire rainy season.  整个雨季她都忙于野外调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hopping</td><td>/ˈhɑːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙碌不停的；跳跃的</td><td>keep the kitchen hopping<br>让厨房忙个不停</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The little diner was hopping from dawn until midnight.  这家小餐馆从清晨忙到午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tied-up</td><td>/ˈtaɪd ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙得不可开交的；被占用的</td><td>tied-up all afternoon<br>整个下午都很忙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager is tied-up in meetings until late tonight.  经理要开会一直忙到今晚深夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 idle</td><td>/ˈaɪdl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲置的，未使用的；懒散的</td><td>idle machines<br>闲置的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Half the factory's machines stood idle for most of last year.  这家工厂一半的机器去年大部分时间都闲置着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inactive</td><td>/ɪnˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不活动的，不活跃的</td><td>remain inactive for months<br>数月毫无动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has been inactive for over two hundred years.  这座火山已有两百多年没有活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unbusy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈbɪzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忙的，空闲的</td><td>an unbusy afternoon<br>空闲的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Call me on an unbusy morning and we can talk properly.  哪天上午不忙时打给我，我们好好聊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unemployed</td><td>/ˌʌnemˈplɔɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>失业的；未被利用的</td><td>unemployed factory workers<br>失业的工厂工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has been unemployed since the plant closed last spring.  自去年春天工厂关闭后他一直失业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unoccupied</td><td>/ʌnˈɑːkjupaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空闲的；无人占用的</td><td>an unoccupied seat<br>无人就座的座位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The upstairs rooms remained unoccupied for the whole winter.  楼上的房间整个冬天都空着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dilute</b></td><td>/daɪˈluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>稀释，冲淡；经稀释的</td><td>dilute the acid<br>稀释这种酸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please dilute the concentrated juice with three parts water before serving it.  上桌前请用三倍的水稀释这种浓缩果汁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thin</td><td>/ˈθɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>使变稀；薄的，瘦的</td><td>thin the paint slightly<br>把颜料稍微稀释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thin the sauce with a little warm water before serving.  上菜前用少量温水把酱汁调稀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 condense</td><td>/kənˈdens/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>压缩，缩短；使凝结</td><td>condense the article<br>压缩这篇文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She managed to condense three chapters into a single clear page.  她把三章内容压缩成清晰的一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 densify</del></td><td>/ˈdɛnsɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使致密，使变浓</td><td>densify the material<br>使材料变致密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers densify the powder before pressing it into blocks.  工程师在压块前先把粉末致密化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concentrate</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsntreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>浓缩；聚集</td><td>concentrate the solution<br>浓缩溶液</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Boil the syrup slowly in order to concentrate the solution.  慢慢熬煮糖浆以便浓缩溶液。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thicken</td><td>/ˈθɪkən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变稠，变浓</td><td>thicken the soup<br>把汤煮稠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stir in a little flour to thicken the gravy.  拌入少许面粉让肉汁变稠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diminish</b></td><td>/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）减少；轻视，贬低</td><td>diminish in importance<br>重要性下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence diminished steadily after he left the board.  他离开董事会后影响力持续减弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abate</td><td>/əˈbeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减轻，减弱；减少</td><td>the storm gradually abated<br>风暴逐渐减弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fever finally abated after two days of medication.  服药两天后，烧终于退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dwindle</td><td>/ˈdwɪndl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>逐渐减少</td><td>dwindle to nothing<br>逐渐减少至无</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their savings dwindled during the months of unemployment.  失业的几个月里他们的积蓄逐渐减少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lessen</td><td>/ˈlesən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减少，减轻</td><td>lessen the risk<br>减少风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple exercises lessen the pain in his stiff shoulder.  简单的锻炼减轻了他僵硬肩膀的疼痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lower</td><td>/ˈloʊər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>降低，减少；较低的</td><td>lower the temperature<br>降低温度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank lowered interest rates twice in one year.  银行一年内两次降低利率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reduce</td><td>/rəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减少，降低</td><td>reduce the cost sharply<br>大幅降低成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New machines reduced the factory's energy use by half.  新机器把工厂的能耗降低了一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decline</td><td>/dɪˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝；下降，衰退</td><td>decline an invitation<br>谢绝邀请</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She politely declined the invitation to speak at the ceremony.  她礼貌地谢绝了在典礼上发言的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belittle</td><td>/bɪˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低，轻视</td><td>belittle her achievements<br>贬低她的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't belittle her achievements just because she works quietly.  别因为她默默工作就贬低她的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deprecate</td><td>/ˈdeprəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成，反对；贬低</td><td>deprecate his efforts<br>贬低他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tends to deprecate his own achievements whenever anyone praises them.  每当有人称赞，他总倾向于贬低自己的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depreciate</td><td>/dɪˈpriːʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低…的价值</td><td>depreciate his contribution<br>贬低他的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not depreciate the value of patience in such long negotiations.  不要贬低耐心在这样漫长谈判中的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disparage</td><td>/dɪˈspærɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低；轻蔑地说</td><td>disparage someone's efforts<br>贬低某人的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not disparage her work simply because she is young.  不要仅仅因为她年轻就贬低她的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aggrandize</td><td>/əˈɡrændaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大（权力、财富、声望）</td><td>aggrandize one's power<br>扩张自己的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor used the crisis to aggrandize his own power.  市长利用危机扩大自己的权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amplify</td><td>/ˈæmpləˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大，增强；详述</td><td>amplify a signal<br>放大信号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Could you amplify your last remark a little?  你能把最后那句话再详述一下吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 augment</td><td>/ɔɡˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>增加，增强</td><td>augment one's income<br>增加收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She augments her salary by teaching evening classes.  她通过教夜校课程增加收入。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boost</td><td>/ˈbuːst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>提高，促进；推动</td><td>boost sales<br>促进销售</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax cut is expected to boost consumer spending.  减税预计会促进消费者支出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enlarge</td><td>/ɪnˈlɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，增大</td><td>enlarge the photograph<br>放大照片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They enlarged the kitchen by removing one interior wall.  他们拆掉一道内墙把厨房扩大了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 escalate</td><td>/ˈeskəleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）（战争等）升级，扩大</td><td>escalate into violence<br>升级为暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The border dispute quickly escalated into a full-scale armed conflict.  边界争端迅速升级为全面武装冲突。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expand</td><td>/ɪkˈspænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，扩张；详述</td><td>expand the business<br>扩大业务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to expand into three new markets.  公司计划扩张到三个新市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 increase</td><td>/ɪnˈkriːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>增加，增长</td><td>increase production sharply<br>大幅提高产量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales increased steadily after the store extended its opening hours.  延长营业时间后销量稳步增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 balloon</td><td>/bəˈluːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>迅速增加</td><td>costs have ballooned<br>成本迅速膨胀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repair costs ballooned from two thousand to twenty thousand dollars.  维修费用从两千美元猛增到两万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disquisition</b></td><td>/ˌdɪskwɪˈzɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专题论文</td><td>a long disquisition on art<br>关于艺术的长篇论述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He delivered a long disquisition on art that lasted well past midnight.  他就艺术作了长篇论述，一直讲到午夜之后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delving</td><td>/ˈdelvɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>深入探究，钻研</td><td>delving into old records<br>钻研旧档案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patient delving into archives uncovered the forgotten letters.  她耐心钻研档案，发现了被遗忘的信件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inquiry</td><td>/ɪnˈkwaɪəri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>调查；询问</td><td>a formal inquiry<br>正式的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parliament ordered an inquiry into the collapse of the bridge.  议会下令调查那座桥的倒塌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 examination</td><td>/ɪɡˌzæməˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>检查，审查；考试</td><td>a close examination<br>仔细的审查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A close examination of the ledger revealed two false entries.  仔细审查账簿发现了两笔假账。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exploration</td><td>/ˌekspləˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>探索，考察</td><td>the exploration of a theme<br>对某主题的探索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book is a careful exploration of rural childhood memory.  这本书是对乡村童年记忆的细致探索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inquisition</td><td>/ˌɪnkwəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉的审问；调查</td><td>a harsh inquisition<br>严厉的审问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The interview felt less like a chat than an inquisition.  这次面谈不像聊天，倒像审问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 probing</td><td>/ˈproʊbɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>探究的，深入的</td><td>a probing question<br>深入的追问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her probing questions forced the witness to explain everything.  她深入的追问迫使证人解释一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dingy</b></td><td>/ˈdɪndʒi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的；肮脏的</td><td>a dingy little room<br>昏暗肮脏的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rented a dingy little room whose single window faced a brick wall.  他们租了一间昏暗肮脏的小屋，唯一的窗户对着一堵砖墙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 darkened</td><td>/ˈdɑːrkənd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>变暗的，昏暗的</td><td>a darkened hallway<br>昏暗的走廊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We felt along the darkened hallway toward the narrow stairs.  我们沿着昏暗的走廊摸向窄楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bedraggled</td><td>/bɪˈdræɡəld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>湿透脏污的，破旧不整的</td><td>a bedraggled coat<br>又湿又脏的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bedraggled dog waited patiently outside the bakery door.  那只又湿又脏的狗耐心等在面包店门外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bemired</del></td><td>/bɪˈmaɪrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满是泥污的</td><td>bemired boots<br>满是泥污的靴子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bemired cart could not move another single meter.  沾满泥污的车再也挪不动一米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besmirched</td><td>/bɪˈsmɜrtʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被弄脏的；名声受损的</td><td>a besmirched reputation<br>受损的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless lie left his besmirched name beyond repair.  一句大意的谎言让他受损的名声无法挽回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dusty</td><td>/ˈdʌsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满是灰尘的</td><td>dusty old books<br>满是灰尘的旧书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We opened dusty boxes in the attic all afternoon.  我们整个下午在阁楼打开满是灰尘的箱子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mucky</td><td>/ˈmʌki/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>污秽的，泥污的</td><td>a mucky farmyard<br>泥污的农家院</td></tr>
