<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their boots were mucky after crossing the flooded field.  穿过被淹的田地后他们的靴子满是污泥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddy</td><td>/ˈmʌdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>浑浊的，不清晰的；使难以理解</td><td>muddy the argument<br>把论点搅浑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vague statistics only muddy the argument instead of settling it.  含糊的统计数据只会把论点搅浑，而不能解决争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smudged</td><td>/smʌdʒd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有污迹的，涂糊的</td><td>smudged ink<br>糊掉的墨迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smudged address made the letter impossible to deliver.  糊掉的地址让这封信无法投递。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soiled</td><td>/ˈsɔɪld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>弄脏的，污损的</td><td>soiled linen<br>脏污的床单</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses collected the soiled sheets early every single morning.  护士每天清早收走脏污的床单。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stained</td><td>/ˈsteɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有污渍的；着色的</td><td>a stained tablecloth<br>有污渍的桌布</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hid the stained shirt at the back of the drawer.  他把有污渍的衬衫藏在抽屉最里面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sullied</td><td>/ˈsʌlid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被玷污的，受损的</td><td>a sullied reputation<br>被玷污的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sullied reputation never fully recovered after the scandal.  丑闻之后他被玷污的名声再也没有完全恢复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clean</td><td>/ˈkliːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干净的；打扫，清洗</td><td>a clean white shirt<br>干净的白衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep the wound clean and change the bandage every day.  保持伤口干净，每天更换绷带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cleanly</td><td>/ˈklenli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>爱清洁的；干净利落地</td><td>cleanly habits<br>爱清洁的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cats are famously cleanly animals that groom themselves constantly.  猫是出名爱清洁的动物，不停梳理自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immaculate</td><td>/ɪˈmækjələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>洁净无瑕的；完美无缺的</td><td>an immaculate record<br>毫无瑕疵的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After thirty years of service his record remained absolutely immaculate.  服务三十年后，他的记录依然毫无瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spotless</td><td>/ˈspɑːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一点污迹也没有的</td><td>a spotless kitchen<br>一尘不染的厨房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her spotless kitchen shone under the bright evening light.  她一尘不染的厨房在明亮的夜灯下发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stainless</td><td>/ˈsteɪnləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；不锈的</td><td>stainless steel pots<br>不锈钢锅具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stainless surface showed not one mark after cooking.  烹饪后这块不锈表面没有一点痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unsoiled</del></td><td>/ˌʌnˈsɔɪld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被弄脏的，洁净的</td><td>unsoiled bandages<br>洁净未污的绷带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only one unsoiled napkin remained by the end of dinner.  晚餐结束时只剩一张未弄脏的餐巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unstained</td><td>/ʌnˈsteɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；清白的</td><td>an unstained record<br>毫无污点的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He retired after forty unstained years of public service.  他在四十年毫无污点的公职之后退休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsullied</td><td>/ʌnˈsʌlid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被玷污的，纯洁的</td><td>an unsullied reputation<br>未被玷污的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unsullied honesty made her the obvious choice for treasurer.  她未受玷污的诚实使她成为财务的当然人选。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diocesan</b></td><td>/daɪˈɑːsɪsn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>主教管辖区的</td><td>diocesan boundaries<br>主教教区的边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bishop redrew the diocesan boundaries to serve the growing suburban parishes.  主教重划了教区边界，以服务日益扩大的郊区堂区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ecumenical</td><td>/ˌiːkjuːˈmenɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>世界范围的，普遍性的</td><td>an ecumenical council<br>世界性的宗教会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ecumenical council brought together leaders from every branch of the church.  一次世界性的宗教会议把教会各支派的领袖聚在一起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>diplomatic</b></td><td>/ˌdɪpləˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>使用策略的，机智的</td><td>a diplomatic reply<br>得体机智的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her diplomatic reply satisfied both sides without conceding anything of real importance.  她机智得体的回答让双方都满意，却没让出任何实质利益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 politic</td><td>/ˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合时宜的，明智的，高情商的</td><td>a politic silence<br>明智的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would not be politic to mention his failure at the dinner.  在晚宴上提他的失败并不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gauche</td><td>/ɡoʊʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，缺乏社交经验的</td><td>a gauche remark<br>笨拙失礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His gauche remark about her salary embarrassed the whole table.  他关于她薪水的笨拙失礼的话让整桌人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impolitic</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不策略的</td><td>an impolitic question<br>不策略的提问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was impolitic to mention his failure at the party.  在聚会上提他的失败很不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tactless</td><td>/ˈtæktləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，笨拙的</td><td>a tactless comment<br>不得体的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One tactless comment about her age spoiled the whole evening.  一句关于她年龄的不得体评论毁了整个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undiplomatic</td><td>/ˌʌndɪpləˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不圆通的，不得体的</td><td>an undiplomatic refusal<br>不得体的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undiplomatic email offended half the department at once.  他不得体的邮件一下惹恼了半个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 untactful</del></td><td>/ˌʌnˈtæktfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，不圆通的</td><td>an untactful question<br>不圆通的提问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her untactful praise embarrassed everyone at the small table.  她不圆通的夸奖让小桌上的人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dire</b></td><td>/ˈdaɪər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，恐怖的；迫切的</td><td>dire consequences<br>可怕的后果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report warned of dire consequences if the dam were not repaired soon.  报告警告说，若不尽快修复大坝，后果将十分可怕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alarming</td><td>/əˈlɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人惊恐的，骇人的</td><td>an alarming increase<br>令人担忧的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alarming report described rivers drying up each summer.  这份骇人的报告描述河流每年夏天干涸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 direful</del></td><td>/ˈdaɪrfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，凄惨的</td><td>direful warnings<br>可怕的警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophet issued direful warnings about the coming winter.  老先知对将至的冬天发出可怕的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dread</td><td>/dred/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恐惧</td><td>fill him with dread<br>使他充满恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thought of another interview filled him with dread all weekend.  一想到还要再面试一次，他整个周末都充满恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fearsome</td><td>/ˈfɪrsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人畏惧的</td><td>a fearsome reputation<br>令人畏惧的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer had a fearsome reputation long before this fight.  这名拳手在此战之前早已令人畏惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forbidding</td><td>/fərˈbɪdɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人生畏的，冷峻的</td><td>a forbidding cliff<br>令人生畏的悬崖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The forbidding gray walls discouraged even the most curious tourists.  冷峻的灰墙让最好奇的游客都望而止步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 formidable</td><td>/ˈfɔːrmɪdəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以对付的，令人敬畏的</td><td>a formidable opponent<br>难以对付的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a formidable opponent in the final round.  她在最后一轮遇到了难以对付的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frightening</td><td>/ˈfraɪtənɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人害怕的</td><td>a frightening noise<br>令人害怕的声响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frightening crack came from somewhere under the ice.  冰层下某处传来令人害怕的开裂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frightful</td><td>/ˈfraɪtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；极糟的</td><td>a frightful accident<br>可怕的事故</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm did frightful damage to the small fishing harbor.  风暴给这个小渔港造成可怕的破坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghastly</td><td>/ˈɡæstli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；惨白的</td><td>a ghastly discovery<br>可怕的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned a ghastly white when he heard the news.  他听到消息时脸色惨白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hair-raising</td><td>/ˈhɛr ˌreɪzɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人毛骨悚然的</td><td>a hair-raising ride<br>令人毛骨悚然的一程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a hair-raising story about the flooded coal mine.  她讲了一个关于被淹煤矿的骇人故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrendous</td><td>/ˌhɔːˈrendəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，极糟的</td><td>horrendous traffic<br>糟糕透顶的交通</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team suffered a horrendous defeat in the opening match.  球队在首场比赛中惨败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrible</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟糕的</td><td>a horrible mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup tasted horrible but nobody dared say so.  汤的味道很糟，但没人敢说出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrifying</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊恐惧的</td><td>horrifying footage<br>令人震惊的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rescuers described horrifying scenes inside the collapsed apartment block.  救援人员描述了倒塌公寓楼内令人震惊的景象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intimidating</td><td>/ɪnˈtɪmɪdeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人畏惧的，恐吓的</td><td>an intimidating silence<br>令人发怵的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner's intimidating stare made the candidate forget everything.  主考官令人发怵的注视让考生忘了一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redoubtable</td><td>/rɪˈdaʊtəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，值得尊敬的；可怕的</td><td>a redoubtable rival<br>令人敬畏的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a redoubtable rival in the final round of the tournament.  她在锦标赛决赛轮中遇上一位令人敬畏的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scary</td><td>/ˈskeri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，吓人的</td><td>a scary movie<br>恐怖电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rope bridge looked scary but felt perfectly solid underfoot.  那座绳桥看着吓人，踩上去却很稳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shocking</td><td>/ˈʃɑːkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊的；骇人的</td><td>shocking news<br>令人震惊的消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographs revealed shocking conditions inside the old prison.  这些照片揭露了旧监狱内骇人的条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrible</td><td>/ˈterəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；糟糕的</td><td>a terrible storm<br>可怕的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lost everything in a terrible fire last winter.  去年冬天一场可怕的火灾让他们失去一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrifying</td><td>/ˈterəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人恐惧的</td><td>a terrifying experience<br>令人恐惧的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Landing in that storm was the most terrifying moment yet.  在那场风暴中降落是至今最恐怖的时刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immediate</td><td>/ɪˈmiːdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的；立即的</td><td>an immediate need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village has an immediate need for clean drinking water.  这个村子迫切需要干净的饮用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burning</td><td>/ˈbɜːrnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的；燃烧的</td><td>a burning question<br>迫切的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Housing remains the burning issue in this crowded district.  住房仍是这个拥挤城区最迫切的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compelling</td><td>/kəmˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极具说服力的</td><td>a compelling reason<br>令人信服的理由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a compelling reason for leaving the project early.  他给出了提前离开项目的极具说服力的理由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crying</td><td>/ˈkraɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的，急需的；哭喊的</td><td>a crying need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a crying need for more rural doctors.  乡村医生的需求十分迫切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emergent</td><td>/ɪˈmɜːrdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，突发的；新兴的</td><td>an emergent crisis<br>突发的危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors must act fast in an emergent situation like this one.  在这样的紧急情况下医生必须迅速行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exigent</td><td>/ˈeksɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的；苛求的</td><td>exigent circumstances<br>紧急情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exigent shortage of water forced the city to ration supplies.  紧急的缺水迫使该市实行配给。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperative</td><td>/ɪmˈperətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>命令的，强制性的；迫切的</td><td>an imperative need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is imperative that we repair the dam before the rains arrive.  在雨季来临前修好大坝是迫切必需的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 importunate</td><td>/ɪmˈpɔrtʃənət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的；胡缠的，纠缠不休的</td><td>importunate demands<br>纠缠不休的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The importunate beggar followed us down the whole street.  那个纠缠不休的乞丐跟着我们走了一整条街。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instant</td><td>/ˈɪnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>紧急的，迫切的；瞬间</td><td>in instant need<br>急需之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter spoke of an instant need for medical supplies.  信中提到对医疗物资的紧急需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 necessitous</del></td><td>/nəˈsɛsɪtəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；紧急的</td><td>necessitous families<br>贫困的家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity was founded to help necessitous widows and orphans.  该慈善机构成立时旨在帮助贫困的寡妇和孤儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pressing</td><td>/ˈpresɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，迫切的</td><td>a pressing problem<br>紧迫的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Housing remains the most pressing issue for the new mayor.  住房仍是新市长最紧迫的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 urgent</td><td>/ˈɜːrdʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，急迫的</td><td>an urgent request<br>紧急的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent an urgent message asking for immediate help.  她发了一条紧急消息请求立刻援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonurgent</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɜrdʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不紧急的</td><td>nonurgent cases<br>非紧急的病例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nonurgent surgeries were postponed until the following month.  非紧急的手术被推迟到了下个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 noncritical</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关键的，不危急的</td><td>noncritical patients<br>病情不危急的病人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noncritical systems can be shut down during the upgrade.  非关键系统可以在升级期间关闭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>demography</b></td><td>/dɪˈmɑːɡrəfi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>人口统计学</td><td>changes in demography<br>人口结构的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Shifts in demography will reshape the country's schools within twenty years.  人口结构的变化将在二十年内重塑这个国家的学校。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disabuse</b></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈbjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打消错误念头，纠正</td><td>disabuse him of the notion<br>打消他的错误念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disabuse him of the notion that the test was simple.  花了好几个小时才打消他认为考试很简单的错误念头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disenchant</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使清醒</td><td>disenchant the young voters<br>使年轻选民清醒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years in politics disenchanted her with grand promises.  多年从政使她对宏大承诺不再抱幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 undeceive</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈsiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使醒悟，使不再受骗</td><td>undeceive the public<br>使公众醒悟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone must undeceive her before she invests everything in that scheme.  必须有人让她醒悟，别把全部钱投进那个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lead into error</td><td>/liːd ˈɪntu ˈɛrər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>引入错误，使误入歧途</td><td>lead students into error<br>把学生引入错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless translation can lead readers into error about the author's meaning.  草率的翻译会使读者误解作者的意思。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disaffected</b></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满的，叛逆的</td><td>disaffected young people<br>心生不满的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Disaffected young workers left the union and formed a committee of their own.  心生不满的年轻工人退出工会，自行组成了一个委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discontented</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈtentɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满足的，不满意的</td><td>discontented workers<br>不满的工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Low pay left the staff increasingly discontented with management.  低薪使员工对管理层越来越不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissatisfied</td><td>/dɪˈsætəsˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不满意的</td><td>dissatisfied with the service<br>对服务不满意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many readers were dissatisfied with the book's ending.  许多读者对这本书的结局不满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malcontent</td><td>/ˌmælkənˈtent/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不满分子；不满的</td><td>a group of malcontents<br>一群不满分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general dismissed the critics as a few malcontents.  将军把批评者说成是几个不满分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebellious</td><td>/rɪˈbeljəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反叛的，难管束的</td><td>a rebellious teenager<br>叛逆的少年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her rebellious streak showed early, long before high school.  她的叛逆性格早早就显露，远在高中之前。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insubordinate</td><td>/ɪnsəˈbɔːrdənət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不服从的，抗命的</td><td>insubordinate behavior<br>抗命的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was discharged for insubordinate conduct at sea.  这名军官因在海上抗命行为被开除。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contumacious</del></td><td>/ˌkɑːntuˈmeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不服从的，倔强的</td><td>a contumacious witness<br>不服从的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contumacious witness was fined for defying the judge.  那名抗命的证人因违抗法官而被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dissatisfy</del></td><td>/dɪˈsætəsˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不满，使不快</td><td>dissatisfy the customers<br>使顾客不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague reply seemed only to dissatisfy the audience further.  含糊的回答似乎只让听众更加不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitate</td><td>/ˈædʒɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>煽动，激起；使不安</td><td>agitate for reform<br>鼓动改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students agitated for changes to the exam system.  学生们为改革考试制度而奔走呼吁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upset</td><td>/əpˈset/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使心烦，使不安；打乱</td><td>upset the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of the closure upset the staff more than management expected.  停业的消息比管理层预想的更让员工不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satisfied</td><td>/ˈsætəsˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>满意的，满足的</td><td>a satisfied customer<br>满意的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked satisfied with the results of the experiment.  她对实验结果看上去很满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contented</td><td>/kənˈtentəd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满足的，知足的</td><td>a contented smile<br>满足的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lived a quiet, contented life in the countryside.  他在乡下过着平静而满足的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disarm</b></td><td>/dɪsˈɑːrm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使息怒，消除敌意；解除武装</td><td>disarm his critics<br>平息批评者的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His frank apology disarmed the critics who had come prepared to attack him.  他坦诚的道歉平息了那些原本准备攻击他的批评者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gentle</td><td>/ˈdʒentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，轻柔的</td><td>a gentle voice<br>温柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the door a gentle push and stepped inside.  他轻轻推了一下门，走了进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 anger</td><td>/ˈæŋɡər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使愤怒；愤怒</td><td>anger the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dismissive remarks angered nearly everyone in the room.  他轻蔑的言论几乎激怒了在场所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enrage</td><td>/enˈreɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使愤怒，激怒</td><td>enrage the audience<br>激怒观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flippant apology only enraged the families further.  他轻率的道歉只让那些家庭更加愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incense</td><td>/ɪnˈsens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>incense the public<br>激怒公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal is certain to incense feminists across the country.  这项提案肯定会激怒全国的女权主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 enflame</del></td><td>/ɛnˈfleɪm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使激怒，煽动</td><td>enflame public opinion<br>煽动公众情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rumor only enflamed the anger of the protesters outside.  那个谣言只是激起了外面抗议者的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infuriate</td><td>/ɪnˈfjʊrieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>infuriate the critics<br>激怒批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision to cut pensions infuriated thousands of workers across the country.  削减养老金的决定激怒了全国成千上万的工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 madden</td><td>/ˈmædən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使发狂，使愤怒</td><td>madden the audience<br>使观众暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless delays maddened passengers waiting at the gate.  无休止的延误让在登机口等待的乘客怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 outrage</td><td>/ˈaʊˌtreɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>愤怒；暴行；激怒</td><td>public outrage<br>公众的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict caused outrage among the victim's family and friends.  判决引起受害者家人和朋友的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disarray</b></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，无秩序</td><td>in complete disarray<br>一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office was in complete disarray after the movers left boxes everywhere.  搬运工把箱子扔得到处都是，办公室一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confusion</td><td>/kənˈfjuːʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱；困惑</td><td>a scene of confusion<br>一片混乱的场面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was total confusion at the station after the announcement.  广播之后车站里一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disarrangement</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪndʒmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，杂乱无序</td><td>the disarrangement of papers<br>文件的杂乱无序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disarrangement of the files suggested someone had searched the office.  文件的杂乱表明有人搜查过办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disorderliness</del></td><td>/dɪsˈɔrdərlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，无秩序</td><td>the disorderliness of the crowd<br>人群的无序状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disorderliness of his notes made the argument hard to follow.  他笔记的杂乱让论证难以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorganization</td><td>/dɪˌsɔːrɡənəˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，组织瓦解</td><td>widespread disorganization<br>普遍的混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden resignation caused months of disorganization in the office.  突然的辞职给办公室带来数月的混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 havoc</td><td>/ˈhævək/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大混乱</td><td>wreak havoc on crops<br>对庄稼造成大破坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flood wreaked havoc on roads and bridges throughout the valley.  洪水对整个山谷的道路和桥梁造成了大破坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess</td><td>/ˈmes/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，脏乱；弄乱</td><td>make a mess of it<br>把它弄得一团糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was a terrible mess when the children finished baking.  孩子们烤完点心后厨房一片狼藉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 messiness</td><td>/ˈmɛsinəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>脏乱，混乱</td><td>the messiness of the desk<br>书桌的脏乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He apologized for the messiness of his handwriting in the notes.  他为笔记中字迹的凌乱表示抱歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misorder</del></td><td>/mɪsˈɔrdər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱；错误排列</td><td>the misorder of pages<br>页码的错乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single misorder of the steps ruined the whole experiment.  步骤的一处错序毁了整个实验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddle</td><td>/ˈmʌdəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使混乱，弄糟；混乱</td><td>muddle the facts<br>把事实弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many footnotes muddled the argument of his short essay.  过多脚注把他短文的论证弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muss</td><td>/mʌs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>弄乱；混乱</td><td>muss up her hair<br>弄乱她的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wind mussed his carefully combed hair in seconds.  风几秒钟就吹乱了他精心梳理的头发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shambles</td><td>/ˈʃæmbəlz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，一片狼藉</td><td>in a complete shambles<br>一片狼藉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project was in a shambles after the funding was cut.  资金被削减后项目一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jumble</td><td>/ˈdʒʌmbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，杂乱堆积；使混乱</td><td>a jumble of ideas<br>杂乱的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her drawer held a jumble of receipts, coins, and old keys.  她的抽屉里杂乱地堆着收据、硬币和旧钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 welter</td><td>/ˈweltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>混乱，动乱</td><td>a welter of details<br>杂乱的一堆细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report buried its main point in a welter of small details.  这份报告把要点埋在了杂乱的琐碎细节之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disorganize</del></td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使混乱，扰乱</td><td>disorganize the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm disorganized every flight leaving the airport that day.  风暴打乱了那天所有离港航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disrupt</td><td>/dɪsˈrʌpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扰乱，打乱</td><td>disrupt the meeting<br>扰乱会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Protesters disrupted the ceremony by shouting from the balcony.  抗议者在楼上喊叫，扰乱了典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturb</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，扰乱；使不安</td><td>disturb the peace<br>扰乱安宁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please do not disturb the birds while they are nesting.  鸟类筑巢时请不要打扰它们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hash</td><td>/ˈhæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>弄乱，搞糟；杂乱之物</td><td>make a hash of it<br>把事情搞得一团糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a complete hash of the presentation yesterday.  他昨天把演讲搞得一团糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rumple</td><td>/ˈrʌmpl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打乱，使不整齐</td><td>rumple his hair<br>把他头发弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His aunt always rumpled the little boy's hair when she visited.  他姨妈每次来都会揉乱小男孩的头发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scramble</td><td>/ˈskræmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使混乱，打乱；争抢</td><td>scramble the signal<br>打乱信号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The software scrambles the letters so the message stays private.  该软件打乱字母顺序以保证消息私密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upset</td><td>/əpˈset/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使心烦，使不安；打乱</td><td>upset the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of the closure upset the staff more than management expected.  停业的消息比管理层预想的更让员工不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess up</td><td>/mɛs ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>弄糟，搞乱</td><td>mess up the plan<br>把计划搞乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not mess up the report the night before the deadline.  别在截止前夜把报告搞砸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mix up</td><td>/ˈmɪks ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>混淆，弄乱</td><td>mix up the names<br>把名字弄混</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I always mix up the twins when they wear the same jacket.  这对双胞胎穿同样的外套时我总会认错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 order</td><td>/ˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>秩序，条理；命令</td><td>restore public order<br>恢复公共秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police worked all night to restore order in the city.  警察通宵工作以恢复城市的秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderliness</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>整齐，有序</td><td>the orderliness of the files<br>档案的整齐有序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors admired the orderliness of the small library.  参观者赞叹这座小图书馆的井然有序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disavow</b></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈvaʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拒绝承认，否认</td><td>disavow any knowledge<br>否认知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator moved quickly to disavow any knowledge of the secret payments.  参议员迅速表态，否认知晓那些秘密付款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deny</td><td>/dɪˈnaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；拒绝给予</td><td>flatly deny the charge<br>断然否认指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I do not deny that the plan carries some very real risks.  我不否认这个计划确实带有一些实实在在的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repudiate</td><td>/rɪˈpjuːdieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝接受</td><td>repudiate a claim<br>否认一项主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author repudiated the views quoted in the article.  作者否认了文章中引用的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disaffirm</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈfɜrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，否定；废除</td><td>disaffirm the contract<br>否认合同效力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court may disaffirm a contract signed by a minor.  法院可以否定未成年人签署的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disclaim</td><td>/dɪsˈkleɪm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，拒绝承认</td><td>disclaim responsibility<br>否认责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company quickly disclaimed any link to the failed venture.  公司迅速否认与那个失败项目有任何关联。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disconfirm</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈfɜrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证明为假，否证</td><td>disconfirm the hypothesis<br>否证该假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New data disconfirmed the theory proposed a decade earlier.  新数据否证了十年前提出的理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gainsay</td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negate</td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；否定，取消</td><td>negate the effect<br>抵消效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless error can negate weeks of careful work.  一个粗心的错误能抹掉数周的细致工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acknowledge</td><td>/ækˈnɑːlɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；对…表示感谢</td><td>acknowledge the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor finally acknowledged that the report contained serious errors.  市长最终承认那份报告有严重错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avow</td><td>/əˈvaʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开承认，坦率声明</td><td>avow one's beliefs openly<br>公开表明信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He avowed his support for the strike in front of reporters.  他在记者面前公开表明支持这次罢工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concede</td><td>/kənˈsiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认</td><td>concede the point<br>承认这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally conceded that her opponent had made a fair point.  她最终承认对手说得有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>discern</b></td><td>/dɪˈsɜːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>识别，辨别差异</td><td>discern a pattern<br>辨别出一种规律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even experts could barely discern a pattern in the scattered rainfall data.  就连专家也几乎辨不出这些零散降雨数据中的规律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 behold</td><td>/bɪˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>看见，注视</td><td>behold the sunrise<br>凝望日出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The travelers stopped to behold the valley below them.  旅人们停下来注视脚下的山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 descry</del></td><td>/dɪˈskraɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>远远看见，察觉</td><td>descry a distant sail<br>远远看见一片船帆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the cliff we could descry a boat on the horizon.  从悬崖上我们能看见地平线上的一只小船。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguish</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>区分，辨别；使显著</td><td>distinguish right from wrong<br>分辨是非</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children cannot always distinguish fantasy from reality.  幼儿并不总能区分幻想与现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 espy</td><td>/ɪˈspaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>看见，突然发现</td><td>espy a familiar face<br>看见一张熟悉的面孔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the hilltop we could espy a thin column of smoke.  从山顶上我们能看见一缕细细的炊烟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perceive</td><td>/pərˈsiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>察觉，理解</td><td>perceive a subtle change<br>察觉细微的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She perceived a subtle change in his tone during dinner.  晚餐时她察觉到他语气的微妙变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regard</td><td>/rɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>关注，重视；看待</td><td>have regard for others<br>顾及他人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drives fast with little regard for other people.  他开车很快，几乎不顾及他人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 differentiate</td><td>/ˌdɪfəˈrenʃieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>区分，区别；使不同</td><td>differentiate between species<br>区分不同物种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is hard to differentiate the two brands by taste alone.  仅凭味道很难区分这两个品牌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discriminate</td><td>/dɪˈskrɪmɪneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>区分；歧视</td><td>discriminate between the two<br>区分二者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children can already discriminate between the two shades of blue.  幼儿已经能够区分这两种深浅不同的蓝色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insightful</td><td>/ˈɪnˌsaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有洞察力的，有见识的</td><td>an insightful comment<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her insightful reading of the poem impressed the whole class.  她对这首诗富有洞见的解读让全班佩服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incisive</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏锐的，深刻的</td><td>an incisive analysis<br>深刻的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her incisive questions exposed the weakness of the proposal.  她敏锐的提问暴露了该提案的弱点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perceptible</td><td>/pərˈseptəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可察觉的</td><td>a barely perceptible change<br>几乎察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a barely perceptible change in her tone of voice.  她的语调有一丝几乎察觉不到的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detectable</td><td>/dɪˈtektəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可检测到的，可察觉的</td><td>detectable levels of lead<br>可检测到的铅含量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new sensor finds detectable traces of gas within seconds.  新传感器几秒内就能发现可检测到的气体痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguishable</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可区分的，可辨别的</td><td>clearly distinguishable from others<br>与其他明显可区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins are barely distinguishable except for a small scar.  这对双胞胎除了一道小疤几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confuse</td><td>/kənˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；混淆</td><td>confuse the issue<br>混淆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague instructions confused nearly everyone in the training class.  含糊的说明让培训班几乎所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mix up</td><td>/ˈmɪks ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>混淆，弄乱</td><td>mix up the names<br>把名字弄混</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I always mix up the twins when they wear the same jacket.  这对双胞胎穿同样的外套时我总会认错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 myopic</td><td>/maɪˈɑːpɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏远见的，缺乏辨别能力的</td><td>a myopic policy<br>缺乏远见的政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city's myopic policy on housing ignored problems that residents saw daily.  这座城市在住房上缺乏远见的政策忽视了居民每天都看得见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shortsighted</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtsaɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短视的，缺乏远见的；近视的</td><td>a shortsighted decision<br>短视的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting research funding now would be extremely shortsighted.  现在削减研究经费是极其短视的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indiscernible</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈsɜrnəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以辨别的，看不出的</td><td>an indiscernible difference<br>难以辨别的差异</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in color was almost indiscernible to the naked eye.  颜色的变化用肉眼几乎辨别不出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperceptible</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈseptɪbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>察觉不到的，极微小的</td><td>an imperceptible shift<br>察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in temperature was almost imperceptible to the guests.  客人几乎察觉不到温度的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>discharge</b></td><td>/dɪsˈtʃɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放；解雇</td><td>discharge the prisoner<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court discharged the prisoner immediately after the new evidence appeared.  新证据出现后，法庭立即释放了那名囚犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fire</td><td>/ˈfaɪər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解雇；开火；火</td><td>fire an idle worker<br>解雇懒散的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager fired two clerks after the audit found losses.  审计查出亏损后经理解雇了两名职员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismiss</td><td>/dɪˈsmɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解散，打发走；解雇；不予考虑</td><td>dismiss the class<br>宣布下课</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher dismissed the class ten minutes ahead of time.  老师提前十分钟宣布下课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disenthrall</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɛnˈθrɔl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，使摆脱束缚</td><td>disenthrall the mind<br>解放思想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Education can disenthrall people from inherited prejudice.  教育能使人摆脱继承来的偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emancipate</td><td>/ɪˈmænsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，解除束缚</td><td>emancipate the slaves<br>解放奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new law emancipated the slaves working on the northern plantations.  新法律解放了在北方种植园劳作的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enfranchise</td><td>/ɪnˈfræntʃaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予…权利（例如选举权）；解放</td><td>enfranchise women<br>赋予妇女选举权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The amendment enfranchised millions of citizens for the first time.  该修正案首次赋予数百万公民选举权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 liberate</td><td>/ˈlɪbəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，释放</td><td>liberate the city<br>解放这座城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Allied troops liberated the city in the spring of that year.  盟军在那年春天解放了这座城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 manumit</del></td><td>/ˌmænjuˈmɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放（奴隶）</td><td>manumit his slaves<br>解放他的奴隶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dying planter chose to manumit every slave who had worked his fields.  这位垂死的种植园主决定解放所有为他耕作过的奴隶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spring</td><td>/sprɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>释放，使越狱；跳跃</td><td>spring him from jail<br>把他从监狱里弄出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lawyer managed to spring him from custody within a week.  他的律师在一周内把他从拘留中弄了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unbind</td><td>/ʌnˈbaɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开，松开，释放</td><td>unbind the prisoner<br>解开囚犯的绑缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She knelt down to unbind the wounded man's hands.  她跪下来解开那名伤者被绑的双手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uncage</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkeɪdʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放出笼子，释放</td><td>uncage the birds<br>把鸟放出笼子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volunteers uncaged the rescued parrots at dawn.  志愿者在黎明时把获救的鹦鹉放出笼子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unchain</td><td>/ʌnˈtʃeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解开锁链，释放</td><td>unchain the dog<br>把狗解开链子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper unchained the old elephant and let it wander freely.  饲养员解开老象的链子，让它自由走动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unfetter</del></td><td>/ʌnˈfɛtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解除束缚，使自由</td><td>unfetter the market<br>解除对市场的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform unfettered small firms and let them compete freely.  改革解除了对小企业的束缚，让它们自由竞争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 employ</td><td>/emˈplɔɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>雇用；使用</td><td>employ new staff<br>雇用新员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory employs nearly six hundred workers in this town.  这家工厂在本镇雇用了近六百名工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engage</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>雇用，聘请；从事，参与</td><td>engage a lawyer<br>聘请一位律师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They engaged a local guide to lead them through the forest.  他们聘请了一位当地导游带他们穿过森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hire</td><td>/ˈhaɪər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>雇用；租用</td><td>hire an assistant<br>雇一名助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bakery decided to hire two more people before the holidays.  面包店决定在假期前再雇两个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 take on</td><td>/ˈteɪk ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>雇用；承担</td><td>take on more workers<br>雇用更多工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm plans to take on twenty interns this summer.  公司计划今年夏天招收二十名实习生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sign up</td><td>/ˈsaɪn ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>签约聘用；报名参加</td><td>sign up a new player<br>签下一名新球员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club signed up three young goalkeepers last month.  俱乐部上个月签下了三名年轻门将。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 on</td><td>/ˈɑːn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>介词、副词</td><td>在工作中，在职；在…上</td><td>on duty tonight<br>今晚值班</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two nurses are always on duty in the ward at night.  夜间病房里总有两名护士值班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bind</td><td>/ˈbaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>捆绑，束缚；约束</td><td>bind his hands<br>捆住他的手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty binds both countries to reduce their emissions.  该条约约束两国削减排放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confine</td><td>/kənˈfaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>限制</td><td>confine the discussion<br>限定讨论范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please confine the discussion to the budget for this year.  请把讨论限定在今年的预算上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 enchain</del></td><td>/ɛnˈtʃeɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用链锁住，束缚</td><td>enchain the prisoners<br>用锁链锁住囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old habits can enchain a person more tightly than any rope.  旧习惯对人的束缚比任何绳索都紧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fetter</td><td>/ˈfetər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚</td><td>the fetters of tradition<br>传统的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy family obligations fettered her ambition for many long years.  沉重的家庭责任多年来束缚了她的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disciple</b></td><td>/dɪˈsaɪpl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信徒，门徒，追随者</td><td>a devoted disciple<br>忠诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became a devoted disciple of the old master painter.  他成了这位老画家的忠实追随者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acolyte</td><td>/ˈækəˌlaɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>助手，随从；信徒</td><td>a loyal acolyte<br>忠诚的追随者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator arrived with three acolytes carrying his papers.  参议员带着三名随从进场，他们拿着他的文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adherent</td><td>/ədˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>追随者，拥护者</td><td>a devoted adherent<br>忠实的拥护者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The movement attracted thousands of adherents in a single year.  这场运动一年内就吸引了数千名拥护者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convert</td><td>/kənˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改变，转化</td><td>convert heat into energy<br>把热转化为能量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers converted the abandoned warehouse into a bright public library.  工程师们把废弃的仓库改造成了明亮的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 epigone</del></td><td>/ˈɛpɪɡoʊn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平庸的模仿者，追随者</td><td>a mere epigone<br>不过是个模仿者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the painter as an epigone of the great masters.  批评家把这位画家贬为大师的平庸模仿者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partisan</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>党派偏见的；盲目拥护者</td><td>partisan politics<br>党派偏见的政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly sank into partisan shouting about very old grievances.  辩论很快沦为带党派偏见的叫嚷，纠缠陈年旧怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 votary</del></td><td>/ˈvoʊtəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>崇拜者，信徒</td><td>a votary of art<br>艺术的崇拜者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A lifelong votary of modern art, she visited the museum weekly.  作为现代艺术的终身崇拜者，她每周都去博物馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leader</td><td>/ˈliːdər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>领导者，带头人</td><td>an industry leader<br>行业领军者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became the leader of the movement while still a student.  她还是学生时就成了这场运动的领导者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 coryphaeus</del></td><td>/ˌkɔrɪˈfiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>领唱者；领袖，首领</td><td>the coryphaeus of the chorus<br>合唱队的领唱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He served as coryphaeus, guiding the chorus through every entrance.  他担任领唱，引导合唱队完成每次进场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>discombobulate</b></del></td><td>/ˌdɪskəmˈbɑːbjuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使慌乱</td><td>discombobulate the speaker<br>使演讲者慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected question completely discombobulated the young candidate.  这个意外的问题让年轻候选人彻底慌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upset</td><td>/əpˈset/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使心烦，使不安；打乱</td><td>upset the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of the closure upset the staff more than management expected.  停业的消息比管理层预想的更让员工不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 addle</td><td>/ˈædəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使混乱，使糊涂</td><td>addle his brain<br>把他的头脑搞糊涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two nights without sleep had addled her thinking completely.  两夜没睡完全搞乱了她的思路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befog</del></td><td>/bɪˈfɔːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使模糊，使困惑</td><td>befog the argument<br>使论证变模糊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Technical jargon befogs the argument instead of making it clearer.  技术术语让论证更模糊，而非更清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befuddle</del></td><td>/bɪˈfʌdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使困惑</td><td>befuddle the audience<br>使听众困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long tables of statistics only befuddle the general audience.  长长的统计表格只会使普通听众困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fuddle</del></td><td>/ˈfʌdəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使糊涂，使迷惑</td><td>fuddle the witness<br>把证人问糊涂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer's rapid questions fuddled the nervous witness.  律师快速的提问把紧张的证人问糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gravel</td><td>/ˈɡrævəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使恼怒，使困惑；砾石</td><td>gravel the teacher<br>把老师惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone graveled everyone at the negotiating table.  他自鸣得意的语气惹恼了谈判桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddle</td><td>/ˈmʌdəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使混乱，弄糟；混乱</td><td>muddle the facts<br>把事实弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many footnotes muddled the argument of his short essay.  过多脚注把他短文的论证弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddy</td><td>/ˈmʌdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>浑浊的，不清晰的；使难以理解</td><td>muddy the argument<br>把论点搅浑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vague statistics only muddy the argument instead of settling it.  含糊的统计数据只会把论点搅浑，而不能解决争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mystify</td><td>/ˈmɪstəˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使迷惑</td><td>mystify the experts<br>使专家们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden failure of the engine mystified the mechanics.  发动机的突然故障让机械师们十分困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puzzle</td><td>/ˈpʌzəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使困惑；谜题</td><td>puzzle the doctors<br>使医生们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sudden recovery puzzled the doctors who had treated her.  她的突然康复让治疗过她的医生们感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compose</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使镇定；组成；创作</td><td>compose oneself<br>让自己镇定下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a breath to compose himself before speaking.  他深吸一口气让自己镇定下来才开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>discomfit</b></del></td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬；阻碍</td><td>discomfit an opponent<br>使对手窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blunt question visibly discomfited the confident speaker.  这个直率的问题明显让自信的演讲者局促不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embarrass</td><td>/ɪmˈbærəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬</td><td>embarrass him in public<br>在公众面前使他尴尬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to embarrass her in public and only embarrassed himself.  他想在公众面前让她尴尬，结果只是让自己出丑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abash</del></td><td>/əˈbæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬，使羞愧</td><td>be abashed by praise<br>因受赞扬而羞怯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked abashed when everyone applauded his clumsy speech.  当大家为他笨拙的演讲鼓掌时，他显得很窘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faze</td><td>/feɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使慌乱，使窘迫</td><td>nothing seems to faze him<br>似乎没什么能扰乱他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The huge crowd did not faze the young pianist at all.  庞大的观众丝毫没让这位年轻钢琴家慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluster</td><td>/ˈflʌstər/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使慌乱，使紧张</td><td>get flustered easily<br>容易慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unexpected questions always fluster him during interviews.  面试中意外的问题总让他慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mortify</td><td>/ˈmɔːrtɪˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使羞愧，使难堪</td><td>be mortified with embarrassment<br>羞愧得无地自容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was mortified to find her mistake printed in the report.  发现自己的错误印在报告里，她羞愧难当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonplus</del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnˈplʌs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使困窘</td><td>be completely nonplussed<br>完全不知所措</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her strange reply left the whole panel nonplussed.  她奇怪的回答让整个评审团都困惑不解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rattle</td><td>/ˈrætəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>发出咔嗒声；使慌乱</td><td>rattle the windows<br>把窗户震得作响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passing train rattled every window in the old house.  驶过的火车震得老屋的每扇窗都咔咔作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balk</td><td>/bɔːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>阻碍；不愿接受，拒绝</td><td>balk at the price<br>因价格而退缩</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foil</td><td>/fɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挫败</td><td>foil a plot<br>挫败阴谋</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thwart</td><td>/θwɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，挫败</td><td>thwart an attempt<br>挫败一次尝试</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 checkmate</td><td>/ˈtʃekˌmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使受挫，彻底击败；将死</td><td>checkmate his opponent<br>彻底挫败对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clever amendment checkmated the governor's entire plan.  一项巧妙的修正案彻底挫败了州长的全部计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forward</td><td>/ˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>向前的；前部的</td><td>take a forward step<br>向前迈一步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He leaned forward to hear the quiet speaker better.  他向前倾身以便听清声音轻的讲话者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foster</td><td>/ˈfɔːstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，鼓励，培养</td><td>foster cooperation<br>促进合作</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 further</td><td>/ˈfɜːrðər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>促进，推动；更远的</td><td>further his career<br>促进他的事业</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 promote</td><td>/prəˈmoʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，助长；晋升</td><td>promote economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>discommode</b></del></td><td>/dɪskəˈmoʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，使不便</td><td>discommode the guests<br>给客人带来不便</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope the change of schedule will not discommode the guests too much.  希望日程变动不会给客人带来太多不便。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturb</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，扰乱；使不安</td><td>disturb the peace<br>扰乱安宁</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 trouble</td><td>/ˈtrʌbəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>麻烦，打扰；使烦恼</td><td>trouble you for a moment<br>打扰你一会儿</td></tr>

<tr><td><del>↳近 incommode</del></td><td>/ˌɪnkəˈmoʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不便，打扰</td><td>incommode the neighbors<br>给邻居带来不便</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put out</td><td>/ˈpʊt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使不便，麻烦；扑灭</td><td>put you out<br>麻烦到你</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 assist</td><td>/əˈsɪst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>帮助，协助</td><td>assist with the work<br>协助工作</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 oblige</td><td>/əˈblaɪdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>帮忙，满足要求；使感激</td><td>happy to oblige<br>乐于帮忙</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 favor</td><td>/ˈfeɪvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞同，好评；恩惠</td><td>win public favor<br>赢得公众好评</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>discompose</b></del></td><td>/ˌdɪskəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安；使混乱</td><td>discompose the audience<br>让观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing seemed to discompose the veteran pilot, not even engine failure.  似乎没什么能让这位老飞行员心慌，连引擎故障也不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitate</td><td>/ˈædʒɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>煽动，激起；使不安</td><td>agitate for reform<br>鼓动改革</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bother</td><td>/ˈbɑːðər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打扰，使烦恼；费心</td><td>don't bother me<br>别烦我</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 discomfort</td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfərt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>不适，不安；使不舒服</td><td>cause great discomfort<br>引起极大不适</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shifted in his seat with obvious discomfort.  他明显不自在地在座位上挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disquiet</td><td>/dɪsˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>不安，忧虑；使不安</td><td>growing disquiet<br>日益增长的不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is growing disquiet among parents about the new curriculum.  家长中对新课程的不安情绪日益增长。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distemper</td><td>/dɪsˈtempər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使心烦意乱；（动物）瘟热</td><td>distemper his mood<br>搅乱他的情绪</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 disconcert</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈsɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安，使慌乱</td><td>disconcert the audience<br>使观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long silences disconcerted everyone at the table.  他长时间的沉默让餐桌上所有人都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturb</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，扰乱；使不安</td><td>disturb the peace<br>扰乱安宁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please do not disturb the birds while they are nesting.  鸟类筑巢时请不要打扰它们。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perturb</td><td>/pərˈtɜːrb/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安，使烦扰</td><td>perturb the balance<br>扰乱平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden change of plan perturbed everyone on the team.  计划的突然改变让全队的人都心神不宁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upset</td><td>/əpˈset/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使心烦，使不安；打乱</td><td>upset the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of the closure upset the staff more than management expected.  停业的消息比管理层预想的更让员工不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weird out</td><td>/ˈwɪrd ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使不安，使发毛</td><td>weird me out<br>让我心里发毛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty hospital corridor really weirded us out at night.  夜里空荡的医院走廊让我们直发毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disarrange</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弄乱，扰乱</td><td>disarrange the papers<br>弄乱文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat jumped up and disarranged every card on the table.  猫跳上来，把桌上的卡片全弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disarray</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，无秩序</td><td>in complete disarray<br>一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office was in complete disarray after the movers left boxes everywhere.  搬运工把箱子扔得到处都是，办公室一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disorganize</del></td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使混乱，扰乱</td><td>disorganize the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm disorganized every flight leaving the airport that day.  风暴打乱了那天所有离港航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compose</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使镇定；组成；创作</td><td>compose oneself<br>让自己镇定下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a breath to compose himself before speaking.  他深吸一口气让自己镇定下来才开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 settle</td><td>/ˈsetəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解决，平息；定居</td><td>settle the dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides finally settled their dispute out of court.  双方最终在庭外解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquilize</td><td>/ˈtræŋkwəˌlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使平静，给…服镇静剂</td><td>tranquilize the animal<br>给动物注射镇静剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vet had to tranquilize the frightened horse.  兽医不得不给这匹惊慌的马注射镇静剂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arrange</td><td>/əˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>安排，筹划；整理</td><td>arrange the meeting<br>安排会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She arranged the whole trip in a single busy afternoon.  她在一个忙碌的下午就安排好了整趟行程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 array</td><td>/əˈreɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一批，一系列；排列</td><td>an impressive array<br>蔚为壮观的一批</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displayed an impressive array of ancient coins.  博物馆展出了一批蔚为壮观的古钱币。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 order</td><td>/ˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>秩序，条理；命令</td><td>restore public order<br>恢复公共秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police worked all night to restore order in the city.  警察通宵工作以恢复城市的秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 organize</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>组织，筹划，安排</td><td>organize the conference<br>筹办会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three students organized the conference without any help from staff.  三名学生在没有职工帮助下筹办了这次会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 range</td><td>/ˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排列，整齐排开；范围</td><td>range the books neatly<br>把书整齐排好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ranged the bottles along the shelf by size and color.  他把瓶子按大小和颜色沿着架子排好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disconcert</b></td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈsɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安，使慌乱</td><td>disconcert the audience<br>使观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long silences disconcerted everyone at the table.  他长时间的沉默让餐桌上所有人都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abash</del></td><td>/əˈbæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬，使羞愧</td><td>be abashed by praise<br>因受赞扬而羞怯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked abashed when everyone applauded his clumsy speech.  当大家为他笨拙的演讲鼓掌时，他显得很窘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discomfit</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬；阻碍</td><td>discomfit an opponent<br>使对手窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blunt question visibly discomfited the confident speaker.  这个直率的问题明显让自信的演讲者局促不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discountenance</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkaʊntənəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成；使窘迫</td><td>discountenance such behavior<br>不赞成这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee discountenanced any further spending on the project.  委员会不赞成该项目再有任何支出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mortify</td><td>/ˈmɔːrtɪˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使羞愧，使难堪</td><td>be mortified with embarrassment<br>羞愧得无地自容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was mortified to find her mistake printed in the report.  发现自己的错误印在报告里，她羞愧难当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rattle</td><td>/ˈrætəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>发出咔嗒声；使慌乱</td><td>rattle the windows<br>把窗户震得作响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passing train rattled every window in the old house.  驶过的火车震得老屋的每扇窗都咔咔作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compose</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使镇定；组成；创作</td><td>compose oneself<br>让自己镇定下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a breath to compose himself before speaking.  他深吸一口气让自己镇定下来才开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 settle</td><td>/ˈsetəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解决，平息；定居</td><td>settle the dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides finally settled their dispute out of court.  双方最终在庭外解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquilize</td><td>/ˈtræŋkwəˌlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使平静，给…服镇静剂</td><td>tranquilize the animal<br>给动物注射镇静剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vet had to tranquilize the frightened horse.  兽医不得不给这匹惊慌的马注射镇静剂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>discord</b></td><td>/ˈdɪskɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意见不一致，不和谐</td><td>sow discord<br>挑起不和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single careless remark sowed discord among the previously friendly neighbors.  一句不慎的话在原本和睦的邻居间挑起了不和。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conflict</td><td>/ˈkɑːnflɪkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冲突，矛盾；抵触</td><td>a conflict of interest<br>利益冲突</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge withdrew because of a possible conflict of interest.  法官因可能存在利益冲突而回避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disaccord</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈkɔrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>in disaccord with the plan<br>与计划不一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His testimony was in clear disaccord with the written record.  他的证词与书面记录明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discordance</td><td>/dɪsˈkɔːrdəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>discordance between reports<br>各报告之间的矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a clear discordance between the two witnesses' accounts.  两位证人的叙述之间存在明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disharmony</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不和谐，不一致</td><td>racial disharmony<br>种族间的不和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of disharmony finally split the once famous band.  多年的不和最终使这支曾经著名的乐队解散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissension</td><td>/dɪˈsenʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意见分歧，不和</td><td>internal dissension<br>内部分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Internal dissension over funding split the small research group into two camps.  关于经费的内部分歧把这个小研究组分成了两派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissidence</td><td>/ˈdɪsədəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>异议，不同意</td><td>political dissidence<br>政治异见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime punished even mild dissidence among its writers.  该政权连作家中温和的异见也要惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissonance</td><td>/ˈdɪsənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（音调）不和谐，刺耳；不一致，分歧</td><td>cognitive dissonance<br>认知失调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cognitive dissonance arises when our actions contradict the beliefs we hold.  当行为与所持信念矛盾时，认知失调便产生了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disunion</td><td>/dɪsˈjuːnjən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，不和</td><td>the threat of disunion<br>分裂的威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate over slavery pushed the nation toward disunion.  关于奴隶制的争论把这个国家推向分裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disunity</td><td>/dɪsˈjuːnəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不统一，不和</td><td>disunity within the party<br>党内的不团结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Open disunity among the leaders cost them the election.  领导层公开的不团结使他们输掉了选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 division</td><td>/dɪˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不和；分开，部门</td><td>deep division over policy<br>政策上的深刻分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is deep division within the committee about the budget.  委员会内部在预算上存在深刻分歧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 friction</td><td>/ˈfrɪkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>摩擦；冲突，不和</td><td>friction between them<br>他们之间的摩擦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant friction between the two managers slowed the whole project.  两位经理之间不断的摩擦拖慢了整个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inharmony</del></td><td>/ɪnˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不和谐，不协调</td><td>inharmony among members<br>成员之间不和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Growing inharmony among members finally split the committee.  成员间日增的不和最终分裂了委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schism</td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strife</td><td>/ˈstraɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>冲突，争斗，不和</td><td>civil strife<br>内乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of political strife left the country deeply exhausted.  多年的政治纷争使这个国家极度疲惫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 row</td><td>/raʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>争吵，争执</td><td>have a row with him<br>和他吵一架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a furious row with her landlord about the unpaid repairs.  她和房东就未付款的维修大吵了一架。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divergence</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrdʒəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，分离，背离</td><td>a sharp divergence of views<br>观点的明显分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wide divergence of opinion delayed the final vote.  意见的巨大分歧推迟了最终投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissonant</td><td>/ˈdɪsənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不一致的</td><td>dissonant voices<br>不和谐的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dissonant chord broke the calm mood of the piece.  一个不和谐的和弦打破了乐曲平静的气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cacophonous</td><td>/kəˈkɑfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，不和谐的</td><td>a cacophonous racket<br>刺耳的喧闹声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra produced a cacophonous din while tuning up.  乐队调音时发出刺耳的嘈杂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disharmonic</del></td><td>/ˌdɪshɑrˈmɑnɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不协调的</td><td>a disharmonic chord<br>不和谐的和弦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The composer opened the piece with a deliberately disharmonic chord.  作曲家用一个刻意不和谐的和弦开始这首曲子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disharmonious</del></td><td>/ˌdɪshɑrˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不一致的</td><td>a disharmonious relationship<br>不和睦的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their disharmonious partnership collapsed within a single season.  他们不和的合作关系在一个赛季内就崩溃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreement</td><td>/əˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，同意；协议</td><td>come to an agreement<br>达成协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms signed an agreement to share research costs.  两家公司签署协议分担研究费用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concord</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和睦</td><td>live in concord<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty brought decades of concord between the two states.  该条约给两国带来数十年的和睦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concordance</td><td>/kənˈkɔrdəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和谐；索引</td><td>in concordance with the data<br>与数据一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The results show close concordance with earlier field studies.  结果显示与早期实地研究高度一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consensus</td><td>/kənˈsensəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致同意</td><td>reach a consensus<br>达成一致意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board finally reached a consensus after four hours of debate.  董事会经过四小时辩论终于达成了一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unanimity</td><td>/ˌjuːnəˈnɪməti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>全体一致，一致同意</td><td>rare unanimity<br>难得的一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was surprising unanimity among the usually divided members.  在平时意见分歧的成员中出现了惊人的一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concordant</td><td>/kənˈkɔrdənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，和谐的</td><td>concordant testimony<br>一致的证词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both witnesses gave concordant accounts of that evening.  两位证人对那天晚上的叙述一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmonious</td><td>/hɑːrˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐的，融洽的；悦耳的</td><td>a harmonious relationship<br>和谐的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two neighbors maintained a harmonious relationship for over thirty years.  这两位邻居三十多年来保持着融洽的关系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>discredit</b></td><td>/dɪsˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使丧失名誉；怀疑</td><td>discredit a witness<br>质疑证人可信度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer tried to discredit the witness with old records.  律师试图用旧档案质疑证人的可信度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
